Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ArrayTac is a piezoelectric-driven tactile display that simultaneously renders shape, stiffness, and friction using a 4x4 actuator array with micro-lever amplification and closed-loop control, enabling realistic haptic feedback through vision-to-touch conversion and long-distance tele-palpation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing tactile displays cannot simultaneously render multiple tactile dimensions (shape, stiffness, friction), limiting haptic simulation realism. There's a need for high-fidelity tactile feedback that can deliver holistic tactile experiences for applications like telemedicine and human-computer interaction.

Method: Developed ArrayTac: a 4x4 array of 16 piezoelectric actuator units with three-stage micro-lever mechanisms to amplify micrometer-scale displacements. Implemented Hall sensor-based closed-loop control at end effectors for enhanced response speed and precision. Created two end-to-end pipelines: 1) vision-to-touch framework using multimodal foundation models to convert visual inputs to tactile signals, and 2) real-time tele-palpation system for long-distance operation.

Result: First-time participants accurately identified object shapes and physical properties with high success rates. In tele-palpation experiments over 1,000km, untrained volunteers achieved 100% accuracy in identifying both number and type of tumors in a breast phantom, with precise localization of tumor positions.

Conclusion: ArrayTac pioneers high-fidelity haptic feedback by enabling simultaneous rendering of shape, stiffness, and friction - an unprecedented capability that delivers holistic tactile experiences previously unattainable, with significant implications for telemedicine and human-computer interaction.

Abstract: Human-computer interaction in the visual and auditory domains has achieved considerable maturity, yet machine-to-human tactile feedback remains underdeveloped. Existing tactile displays struggle to simultaneously render multiple tactile dimensions, such as shape, stiffness, and friction, which limits the realism of haptic simulation. Here, we present ArrayTac, a piezoelectric-driven tactile display capable of simultaneously rendering shape, stiffness, and friction to reproduce realistic haptic signals. The system comprises a 4x4 array of 16 actuator units, each employing a three-stage micro-lever mechanism to amplify the micrometer-scale displacement of the piezoelectric element, with Hall sensor-based closed-loop control at the end effector to enhance response speed and precision. We further implement two end-to-end pipelines: 1) a vision-to-touch framework that converts visual inputs into tactile signals using multimodal foundation models, and 2) a real-time tele-palpation system operating over distances of several thousand kilometers. In user studies, first-time participants accurately identify object shapes and physical properties with high success rates. In a tele-palpation experiment over 1,000km, untrained volunteers correctly identified both the number and type of tumors in a breast phantom with 100% accuracy and precisely localized their positions. The system pioneers a new pathway for high-fidelity haptic feedback by introducing the unprecedented capability to simultaneously render an object's shape, stiffness, and friction, delivering a holistic tactile experience that was previously unattainable.

</details>


### [133] [GraspADMM: Improving Dexterous Grasp Synthesis via ADMM Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13832)
*Liangwang Ruan,Jiayi Chen,He Wang,Baoquan Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: GraspADMM is a novel grasp synthesis framework that combines sampling-based diversity with ADMM-based refinement to simultaneously improve kinematic feasibility and dynamic stability, achieving significant performance gains over state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing grasp synthesis methods face limitations: Dexonomy ensures diversity and kinematic feasibility but lacks dynamic stability optimization due to fixed contact points, while gradient-based optimizers maximize dynamic stability but cause physical penetrations due to simplified contact approximations. There's a need to bridge this gap by preserving diversity while improving both kinematic feasibility and dynamic stability.

Method: GraspADMM uses the Alternating Direction Method of Multipliers (ADMM) to decouple target contact points on the object from actual contact locations on the hand. The framework alternates between: 1) updating target object points to maximize dynamic grasp metrics, and 2) adjusting hand pose to physically reach these targets while strictly respecting collision boundaries. This preserves sampling-based diversity while enabling simultaneous optimization of kinematic feasibility and dynamic stability.

Result: GraspADMM significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, achieving nearly 15% absolute improvement in grasp success rate for type-unaware synthesis and roughly 100% relative improvement in type-aware synthesis. The approach maintains robust, physically plausible grasp generation even under extreme low-friction conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed ADMM-based decomposition effectively bridges the gap between sampling-based diversity and gradient-based stability optimization, enabling high-quality dexterous grasp synthesis that simultaneously addresses diversity, kinematic feasibility, and dynamic stability requirements.

Abstract: Synthesizing high-quality dexterous grasps is a fundamental challenge in robot manipulation, requiring adherence to diversity, kinematic feasibility (valid hand-object contact without penetration), and dynamic stability (secure multi-contact forces). The recent framework Dexonomy successfully ensures broad grasp diversity through dense sampling and improves kinematic feasibility via a simulator-based refinement method that excels at resolving exact collisions. However, its reliance on fixed contact points restricts the hand's reachability and prevents the optimization of grasp metrics for dynamic stability. Conversely, purely gradient-based optimizers can maximize dynamic stability but rely on simplified contact approximations that inevitably cause physical penetrations. To bridge this gap, we propose GraspADMM, a novel grasp synthesis framework that preserves sampling-based diversity while improving kinematic feasibility and dynamic stability. By formulating the refinement stage using the Alternating Direction Method of Multipliers (ADMM), we decouple the target contact points on the object from the actual contact locations on the hand. This decomposition allows the pipeline to alternate between updating the target object points to directly maximize dynamic grasp metrics, and adjusting the hand pose to physically reach these targets while strictly respecting collision boundaries. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GraspADMM significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, achieving a nearly 15\% absolute improvement in grasp success rate for type-unaware synthesis and roughly a 100\% relative improvement in type-aware synthesis. Furthermore, our approach maintains robust, physically plausible grasp generation even under extreme low-friction conditions.

</details>


### [134] [ImagiNav: Scalable Embodied Navigation via Generative Visual Prediction and Inverse Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13833)
*Jie Chen,Yuxin Cai,Yizhuo Wang,Ruofei Bai,Yuhong Cao,Jun Li,Yau Wei Yun,Guillaume Sartoretti*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ImagiNav: A modular framework for zero-shot robot navigation using in-the-wild videos, decoupling visual planning from actuation via hierarchical text-to-video imagination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Vision-Language Navigation methods rely on expensive, embodiment-specific robot data and face limitations due to simulation's limited scene diversity and visual fidelity, hindering scaling and generalization.

Method: Hierarchical modular framework: 1) Vision-Language Model decomposes instructions into textual subgoals, 2) Finetuned generative video model imagines future video trajectory towards subgoal, 3) Inverse dynamics model extracts trajectory from imagined video for low-level controller tracking. Includes scalable data pipeline using in-the-wild navigation videos auto-labeled via inverse dynamics and pretrained VLM.

Result: Demonstrates strong zero-shot transfer to robot navigation without requiring robot demonstrations, enabling navigation learning directly from unlabeled, open-world data.

Conclusion: ImagiNav paves the way for generalist robots that learn navigation from diverse in-the-wild videos, overcoming simulation limitations and expensive robot-specific training data requirements.

Abstract: Enabling robots to navigate open-world environments via natural language is critical for general-purpose autonomy. Yet, Vision-Language Navigation has relied on end-to-end policies trained on expensive, embodiment-specific robot data. While recent foundation models trained on vast simulation data show promise, the challenge of scaling and generalizing due to the limited scene diversity and visual fidelity in simulation persists. To address this gap, we propose ImagiNav, a novel modular paradigm that decouples visual planning from robot actuation, enabling the direct utilization of diverse in-the-wild navigation videos. Our framework operates as a hierarchy: a Vision-Language Model first decomposes instructions into textual subgoals; a finetuned generative video model then imagines the future video trajectory towards that subgoal; finally, an inverse dynamics model extracts the trajectory from the imagined video, which can then be tracked via a low-level controller. We additionally develop a scalable data pipeline of in-the-wild navigation videos auto-labeled via inverse dynamics and a pretrained Vision-Language Model. ImagiNav demonstrates strong zero-shot transfer to robot navigation without requiring robot demonstrations, paving the way for generalist robots that learn navigation directly from unlabeled, open-world data.

</details>


### [135] [Fine-tuning is Not Enough: A Parallel Framework for Collaborative Imitation and Reinforcement Learning in End-to-end Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13842)
*Zhexi Lian,Haoran Wang,Xuerun Yan,Weimeng Lin,Xianhong Zhang,Yongyu Chen,Jia Hu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PaIR-Drive: A parallel framework for collaborative imitation and reinforcement learning in autonomous driving that avoids sequential fine-tuning issues by running IL and RL in parallel branches with conflict-free objectives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional end-to-end autonomous driving relies on imitation learning limited by human demonstration quality. Sequential RL fine-tuning introduces policy drift and performance ceilings due to dependence on pretrained IL policies.

Method: Parallel framework separating IL and RL into two branches with conflict-free training objectives. Introduces tree-structured trajectory neural sampler for grouped relative policy optimization (GRPO) in RL branch to enhance exploration.

Result: Achieves competitive performance of 91.2 PDMS and 87.9 EPDMS on NAVSIMv1 and v2 benchmarks using Transfuser and DiffusionDrive IL baselines. Outperforms existing RL fine-tuning methods and can correct human experts' suboptimal behaviors.

Conclusion: PaIR-Drive enables fully collaborative optimization between IL and RL, eliminates need to retrain RL with new IL policies, and allows RL to leverage IL policy for final plan optimization beyond prior knowledge limitations.

Abstract: End-to-end autonomous driving is typically built upon imitation learning (IL), yet its performance is constrained by the quality of human demonstrations. To overcome this limitation, recent methods incorporate reinforcement learning (RL) through sequential fine-tuning. However, such a paradigm remains suboptimal: sequential RL fine-tuning can introduce policy drift and often leads to a performance ceiling due to its dependence on the pretrained IL policy. To address these issues, we propose PaIR-Drive, a general Parallel framework for collaborative Imitation and Reinforcement learning in end-to-end autonomous driving. During training, PaIR-Drive separates IL and RL into two parallel branches with conflict-free training objectives, enabling fully collaborative optimization. This design eliminates the need to retrain RL when applying a new IL policy. During inference, RL leverages the IL policy to further optimize the final plan, allowing performance beyond prior knowledge of IL. Furthermore, we introduce a tree-structured trajectory neural sampler to group relative policy optimization (GRPO) in the RL branch, which enhances exploration capability. Extensive analysis on NAVSIMv1 and v2 benchmark demonstrates that PaIR-Drive achieves Competitive performance of 91.2 PDMS and 87.9 EPDMS, building upon Transfuser and DiffusionDrive IL baselines. PaIR-Drive consistently outperforms existing RL fine-tuning methods, and could even correct human experts' suboptimal behaviors. Qualitative results further confirm that PaIR-Drive can effectively explore and generate high-quality trajectories.

</details>


### [136] [LDHP: Library-Driven Hierarchical Planning for Non-prehensile Dexterous Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13844)
*Tierui He,Chao Zhao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LDHP is a hierarchical planner for non-prehensile manipulation that decomposes tasks into object-pose planning and grasp synthesis, ensuring executability through collision checks and quasi-static mechanics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for non-prehensile manipulation have limitations: planning/search methods rely on ad-hoc designs or generate unrealizable motions by ignoring gripper properties, while training-based approaches are data-intensive and struggle with generalization to novel tasks.

Method: Library-driven hierarchical planner with two tiers: top-tier contact-state planner uses MoveObject primitives for object-pose paths; bottom-tier grasp planner uses AdjustGrasp primitives for feasible grasp sequences. Feasibility is certified via collision checks and quasi-static mechanics, with contact-sensitive segments refined via bounded dichotomy refinement.

Result: Real-robot studies on zero-mobility lifting and slot insertion demonstrate consistent execution and robustness to shape and environment changes. The method transfers across manipulation tasks and geometric variations without re-design.

Conclusion: LDHP's gripper-aware decomposition decouples object motion from grasp realizability, creates a task-agnostic pipeline, and provides clean hooks for optional learned priors, addressing key limitations of prior approaches.

Abstract: Non-prehensile manipulation is essential for handling thin, large, or otherwise ungraspable objects in unstructured settings. Prior planning and search-based methods often rely on ad-hoc manual designs or generate physically unrealizable motions by ignoring critical gripper properties, while training-based approaches are data-intensive and struggle to generalize to novel, out-of-distribution tasks. We propose a library-driven hierarchical planner (LDHP) that makes executability a first-class design goal: a top-tier contact-state planner proposes object-pose paths using MoveObject primitives, and a bottom-tier grasp planner synthesizes feasible grasp sequences with AdjustGrasp primitives; feasibility is certified by collision checks and quasi-static mechanics, and contact-sensitive segments are recovered via a bounded dichotomy refinement. This gripper-aware decomposition decouples object motion from grasp realizability, yields a task-agnostic pipeline that transfers across manipulation tasks and geometric variations without re-design, and exposes clean hooks for optional learned priors. Real-robot studies on zero-mobility lifting and slot insertion demonstrate consistent execution and robustness to shape and environment changes.

</details>


### [137] [TransDex: Pre-training Visuo-Tactile Policy with Point Cloud Reconstruction for Dexterous Manipulation of Transparent Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13869)
*Fengguan Li,Yifan Ma,Chen Qian,Wentao Rao,Weiwei Shang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: TransDex is a 3D visuo-tactile fusion policy using point cloud reconstruction pre-training to address manipulation challenges with transparent objects, outperforming baselines in real-world experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dexterous manipulation faces challenges with self-occlusion, severe depth noise, and depth information loss when handling transparent objects, necessitating improved perception and control methods.

Method: Proposes TransDex with: 1) Self-supervised point cloud reconstruction pre-training using Transformer to recover 3D structure from noisy/masked interactive point clouds; 2) Fine-grained hierarchical perceptual encoding; 3) Multi-round attention mechanisms for adaptive fusion of robotic arm and dexterous hand features for differentiated motion prediction.

Result: TransDex outperforms existing baseline methods in transparent object manipulation experiments on a real robotic system, with further analysis validating generalization capabilities and component effectiveness.

Conclusion: TransDex successfully addresses transparent object manipulation challenges through 3D visuo-tactile fusion with point cloud reconstruction pre-training, demonstrating superior performance and generalization in real-world robotic applications.

Abstract: Dexterous manipulation enables complex tasks but suffers from self-occlusion, severe depth noise, and depth information loss when manipulating transparent objects. To solve this problem, this paper proposes TransDex, a 3D visuo-tactile fusion motor policy based on point cloud reconstruction pre-training. Specifically, we first propose a self-supervised point cloud reconstruction pre-training approach based on Transformer. This method accurately recovers the 3D structure of objects from interactive point clouds of dexterous hands, even when random noise and large-scale masking are added. Building on this, TransDex is constructed in which perceptual encoding adopts a fine-grained hierarchical scheme and multi-round attention mechanisms adaptively fuse features of the robotic arm and dexterous hand to enable differentiated motion prediction. Results from transparent object manipulation experiments conducted on a real robotic system demonstrate that TransDex outperforms existing baseline methods. Further analysis validates the generalization capabilities of TransDex and the effectiveness of its individual components.

</details>


### [138] [Path-conditioned Reinforcement Learning-based Local Planning for Long-Range Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13888)
*Mateo Haro,Julia Richter,Fan Yang,Cesar Cadena,Marco Hutter*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RL-based local navigation policy uses path information as contextual guidance without explicit path-following rewards, improving efficiency with good paths while maintaining robustness to degraded guidance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hierarchical navigation pipelines are sensitive to global path quality - inaccurate remote sensing leads to infeasible waypoints that degrade local execution, while limited global context hinders long-range efficiency.

Method: Reinforcement learning-based local navigation policy conditioned on reference path observations, trained with reward function based on goal-reaching objectives without explicit path-following reward.

Result: Significantly improves navigation efficiency when high-quality paths are available, maintains baseline-level performance when path observations are severely degraded or non-existent.

Conclusion: The approach is well-suited for long-range navigation where high-level plans are approximate and local execution must remain adaptive to uncertainty through implicit conditioning on path information.

Abstract: Long-range navigation is commonly addressed through hierarchical pipelines in which a global planner generates a path, decomposed into waypoints, and followed sequentially by a local planner. These systems are sensitive to global path quality, as inaccurate remote sensing data can result in locally infeasible waypoints, which degrade local execution. At the same time, the limited global context available to the local planner hinders long-range efficiency. To address this issue, we propose a reinforcement learning-based local navigation policy that leverages path information as contextual guidance. The policy is conditioned on reference path observations and trained with a reward function mainly based on goal-reaching objectives, without any explicit path-following reward. Through this implicit conditioning, the policy learns to opportunistically exploit path information while remaining robust to misleading or degraded guidance. Experimental results show that the proposed approach significantly improves navigation efficiency when high-quality paths are available and maintains baseline-level performance when path observations are severely degraded or even non-existent. These properties make the method particularly well-suited for long-range navigation scenarios in which high-level plans are approximate and local execution must remain adaptive to uncertainty.

</details>


### [139] [LineMaster Pro: A Low-Cost Intelligent Line Following Robot with PID Control and Ultrasonic Obstacle Avoidance for Educational Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13907)
*Jeni Shahi,Abhishek Shah,A. S. M. Ahsanul Sarkar Akib*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LineMaster Pro: A $28.50 Arduino-based line following robot with integrated obstacle detection, PID control, and 94% cost reduction compared to commercial alternatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Commercial line following robots are expensive ($150-300) and lack integrated obstacle detection capabilities, limiting accessibility for robotics education, especially in resource-constrained environments.

Method: Arduino Nano platform with dual TCRT5000 infrared sensors for line tracking, HC-SR04 ultrasonic sensor for obstacle detection, digitally tuned PID controller with Ziegler-Nichols optimization, hierarchical finite state machine for obstacle avoidance, and four-phase sensor calibration methodology.

Result: Mean tracking accuracy of 1.18 cm at 0.4 m/s, obstacle detection reliability of 96.7% within 10-40 cm range with 0.7% false positive rate, 94% successful recovery from path deviations, 43% improvement over conventional on-off control, and 94% cost reduction ($28.50 vs $150-300).

Conclusion: LineMaster Pro establishes a practical benchmark for accessible robotics education in resource-constrained environments through its low-cost, high-performance design with integrated obstacle detection capabilities.

Abstract: Line following robots are fundamental platforms in robotics education, yet commercially available solutions remain prohibitively expensive ($150-300$) while lacking integrated obstacle detection capabilities essential for real-world applications. This paper presents LineMaster Pro, an intelligent low-cost line following robot implemented on an Arduino Nano platform that integrates dual TCRT5000 infrared sensors for precision line tracking, an HC-SR04 ultrasonic sensor for real-time obstacle detection, a digitally tuned PID controller with Ziegler-Nichols optimization, and a hierarchical finite state machine for robust obstacle avoidance. A systematic four-phase sensor calibration methodology ensures reliable operation across varying lighting and surface conditions. Experimental validation through 200 controlled trials and 72-hour continuous operation demonstrates mean tracking accuracy of 1.18 cm at 0.4 m/s (95\% CI [1.06, 1.30]), obstacle detection reliability of 96.7\% within 10-40 cm range with 0.7\% false positive rate, and 94\% successful recovery from path deviations. The PID implementation achieves 43\% improvement over conventional on-off control ($p<0.001$). At a total hardware cost of \$28.50 based on verified Bangladesh market prices, LineMaster Pro achieves a 94\% cost reduction compared to commercial alternatives, establishing a practical benchmark for accessible robotics education in resource-constrained environments.

</details>


### [140] [Data-Driven Autoregressive Power Prediction for GTernal Robots in the Robotarium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13908)
*Yassin Abdelmeguid,Ammar Hasan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Lightweight autoregressive MLP achieves accurate power prediction for mobile robots by leveraging temporal autocorrelation, enabling real-time energy-aware multi-robot algorithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing energy-aware algorithms for multi-robot systems rely on kinematic approximations that fail to capture complex real hardware dynamics, creating a need for accurate power consumption models.

Method: Developed a lightweight 7,041-parameter multi-layer perceptron (MLP) that conditions on recent power history to predict power consumption, analyzing 48,000 samples across six motion trials from GTernal robots in Georgia Tech Robotarium.

Result: The model achieves R² = 0.90 on held-out motion patterns, reaching theoretical prediction ceiling imposed by measurement noise, with strong temporal autocorrelation (ρ₁ = 0.95) dominating kinematic effects. Physical validation across seven robots yields mean R² = 0.87 with zero-shot transfer to unseen robots and behaviors.

Conclusion: The autoregressive predictor enables real-time deployment at 150× the platform's 30 Hz control rate (224 μs per inference), providing accurate power models for energy-aware multi-robot algorithm development, with trained model and dataset released.

Abstract: Energy-aware algorithms for multi-robot systems require accurate power consumption models, yet existing approaches rely on kinematic approximations that fail to capture the complex dynamics of real hardware. We present a lightweight autoregressive predictor for the GTernal mobile robot platform deployed in the Georgia Tech Robotarium. Through analysis of 48,000 samples collected across six motion trials, we discover that power consumption exhibits strong temporal autocorrelation ($ρ_1 = 0.95$) that dominates kinematic effects. A 7,041-parameter multi-layer perceptron (MLP) achieves $R^2 = 0.90$ on held-out motion patterns by conditioning on recent power history, reaching the theoretical prediction ceiling imposed by measurement noise. Physical validation across seven robots in a collision avoidance scenario yields mean $R^2 = 0.87$, demonstrating zero-shot transfer to unseen robots and behaviors. The predictor runs in 224 $μ$s per inference, enabling real-time deployment at 150$\times$ the platform's 30 Hz control rate. We release the trained model and dataset to support energy-aware multi-robot algorithm development.

</details>


### [141] [SmoothVLA: Aligning Vision-Language-Action Models with Physical Constraints via Intrinsic Smoothness Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13925)
*Jiashun Li,Xiaoyu Shi,Hong Xie,Mingsheng Shang,Yun Lu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SmoothVLA is a reinforcement learning fine-tuning framework for Vision-Language-Action models that optimizes both task performance and motion smoothness using a physics-informed hybrid reward function, addressing the stability-exploration dilemma in robotic manipulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing post-training methods for VLA models face a dilemma: Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) is constrained by demonstration quality and lacks generalization, while Reinforcement Learning (RL) improves exploration but often produces erratic, jittery trajectories that violate physical constraints. There's a need to bridge this gap between stability and exploration.

Method: SmoothVLA uses a physics-informed hybrid reward function that integrates binary sparse task rewards with a continuous dense term derived from trajectory jerk. This intrinsic reward is computed directly from policy rollouts without requiring extrinsic environment feedback. The framework leverages Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to establish trajectory smoothness as an explicit optimization prior.

Result: Extensive experiments on the LIBERO benchmark show that SmoothVLA outperforms standard RL by 13.8% in smoothness and significantly surpasses SFT in generalization across diverse tasks.

Conclusion: SmoothVLA offers a scalable approach to aligning VLA models with physical-world constraints through intrinsic reward optimization, effectively balancing task performance with motion smoothness for robotic manipulation.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for robotic manipulation. However, existing post-training methods face a dilemma between stability and exploration: Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) is constrained by demonstration quality and lacks generalization, whereas Reinforcement Learning (RL) improves exploration but often induces erratic, jittery trajectories that violate physical constraints. To bridge this gap, we propose SmoothVLA, a novel reinforcement learning fine-tuning framework that synergistically optimizes task performance and motion smoothness. The technical core is a physics-informed hybrid reward function that integrates binary sparse task rewards with a continuous dense term derived from trajectory jerk. Crucially, this reward is intrinsic, that computing directly from policy rollouts, without requiring extrinsic environment feedback or laborious reward engineering. Leveraging the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), SmoothVLA establishes trajectory smoothness as an explicit optimization prior, guiding the model toward physically feasible and stable control. Extensive experiments on the LIBERO benchmark demonstrate that SmoothVLA outperforms standard RL by 13.8\% in smoothness and significantly surpasses SFT in generalization across diverse tasks. Our work offers a scalable approach to aligning VLA models with physical-world constraints through intrinsic reward optimization.

</details>


### [142] [ToMPC: Task-oriented Model Predictive Control via ADMM for Safe Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13944)
*Xinyu Jia,Wenxin Wang,Jun Yang,Yongping Pan,Haoyong Yu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ToMPC framework for robotic manipulation that unifies collision-free motion and robot-environment interaction with task-oriented obstacle avoidance using kinematic redundancy, solved via ADMM decomposition into DDP and QP subproblems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Need for safe and efficient robotic manipulation in open workspaces that can handle diverse scenarios requiring both motion planning and robot-environment interaction, particularly in obstructed environments where traditional approaches may be insufficient.

Method: Task-oriented MPC framework with task-oriented obstacle avoidance leveraging kinematic redundancy. The optimization problem is solved using ADMM, which decomposes it into two subproblems: one solved by differential dynamic programming (DDP) for trajectory optimization, and another solved by quadratic programming (QP) for constraint handling.

Result: Framework validated in simulation and hardware experiments on Franka Panda manipulator. Capable of planning motion and/or force trajectories in real time, maximizing manipulation range while avoiding obstacles, and strictly adhering to safety-related hard constraints.

Conclusion: ToMPC framework successfully addresses safe and efficient robotic manipulation in open workspaces by unifying motion planning and interaction control with task-oriented obstacle avoidance, demonstrating real-time capability and strict constraint satisfaction.

Abstract: This paper proposes a task-oriented model predictive control (ToMPC) framework for safe and efficient robotic manipulation in open workspaces. The framework unifies collision-free motion and robot-environment interaction to address diverse scenarios. Additionally, it introduces task-oriented obstacle avoidance that leverages kinematic redundancy to enhance manipulation efficiency in obstructed environments. This complex optimization problem is solved by the alternating direction method of multipliers (ADMM), which decomposes the problem into two subproblems tackled by differential dynamic programming (DDP) and quadratic programming (QP), respectively. The effectiveness of this approach is validated in simulation and hardware experiments on a Franka Panda robotic manipulator. Results demonstrate that the framework can plan motion and/or force trajectories in real time, maximize the manipulation range while avoiding obstacles, and strictly adhere to safety-related hard constraints.

</details>


### [143] [Vision-guided Autonomous Dual-arm Extraction Robot for Bell Pepper Harvesting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13987)
*Kshitij Madhav Bhat,Tom Gao,Abhishek Mathur,Rohit Satishkumar,Francisco Yandun,Dominik Bauer,Nancy Pollard*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: VADER is a dual-arm mobile robot system for autonomous bell pepper harvesting in outdoor farms, achieving over 60% success rate with under 100-second cycle time, supported by a hierarchical dataset for robust perception.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bell pepper harvesting is labor-intensive (up to 50% of production costs), and existing automated solutions work well only in controlled greenhouse environments but fail in unstructured outdoor farms due to environmental variability and occlusion.

Method: VADER integrates a robust perception pipeline with a dual-arm planning framework coordinating a gripping arm and cutting arm for extraction. Uses a hierarchical dataset (3,200+ images) with coarse-to-fine training strategy from fruit detection to high-precision pose estimation, and includes teleoperation fail-safe via GELLO framework.

Result: System achieves harvest success rate exceeding 60% with cycle time under 100 seconds per fruit in various realistic outdoor conditions. Dataset and code will be made publicly available.

Conclusion: VADER demonstrates feasibility of autonomous bell pepper harvesting in outdoor environments, addressing key challenges of environmental variability and occlusion through integrated perception and dual-arm manipulation with teleoperation backup.

Abstract: Agricultural robotics has emerged as a critical solution to the labor shortages and rising costs associated with manual crop harvesting. Bell pepper harvesting, in particular, is a labor-intensive task, accounting for up to 50% of total production costs. While automated solutions have shown promise in controlled greenhouse environments, harvesting in unstructured outdoor farms remains an open challenge due to environmental variability and occlusion. This paper presents VADER (Vision-guided Autonomous Dual-arm Extraction Robot), a dual-arm mobile manipulation system designed specifically for the autonomous harvesting of bell peppers in outdoor environments. The system integrates a robust perception pipeline coupled with a dual-arm planning framework that coordinates a gripping arm and a cutting arm for extraction. We validate the system through trials in various realistic conditions, demonstrating a harvest success rate exceeding 60% with a cycle time of under 100 seconds per fruit, while also featuring a teleoperation fail-safe based on the GELLO teleoperation framework to ensure robustness. To support robust perception, we contribute a hierarchically structured dataset of over 3,200 images spanning indoor and outdoor domains, pairing wide-field scene images with close-up pepper images to enable a coarse-to-fine training strategy from fruit detection to high-precision pose estimation. The code and dataset will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

</details>


### [144] [URDF-Anything+: Autoregressive Articulated 3D Models Generation for Physical Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14010)
*Zhuangzhe Wu,Yue Xin,Chengkai Hou,Minghao Chen,Yaoxu Lyu,Jieyu Zhang,Shanghang Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: An end-to-end autoregressive framework that generates executable articulated object models (URDFs) from visual observations, enabling direct sim-to-real transfer without online adaptation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Articulated object reconstruction from visual input is challenging as it requires jointly inferring both part geometry and kinematic structure. Current methods lack end-to-end solutions for generating executable models directly from observations.

Method: End-to-end autoregressive framework that takes image and object-level 3D cues as input, then sequentially generates part geometries and associated joint parameters until all parts are produced, resulting in complete URDF models without multi-stage pipelines.

Result: Outperforms prior methods on large-scale articulated object benchmarks and real-world robotic tasks in geometric reconstruction quality, joint parameter accuracy, and physical executability.

Conclusion: The framework enables a new Real-Follow-Sim paradigm where high-fidelity digital twins constructed from visual observations allow policies trained purely in simulation to transfer to real robots without online adaptation.

Abstract: Articulated objects are fundamental for robotics, simulation of physics, and interactive virtual environments. However, reconstructing them from visual input remains challenging, as it requires jointly inferring both part geometry and kinematic structure. We present, an end-to-end autoregressive framework that directly generates executable articulated object models from visual observations. Given image and object-level 3D cues, our method sequentially produces part geometries and their associated joint parameters, resulting in complete URDF models without reliance on multi-stage pipelines. The generation proceeds until the model determines that all parts have been produced, automatically inferring complete geometry and kinematics. Building on this capability, we enable a new Real-Follow-Sim paradigm, where high-fidelity digital twins constructed from visual observations allow policies trained and tested purely in simulation to transfer to real robots without online adaptation. Experiments on large-scale articulated object benchmarks and real-world robotic tasks demonstrate that outperforms prior methods in geometric reconstruction quality, joint parameter accuracy, and physical executability.

</details>


### [145] [Amortizing Trajectory Diffusion with Keyed Drift Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14056)
*Gokul Puthumanaillam,Melkior Ornik*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: KDP enables diffusion-like trajectory planning with one-step inference by using conditioning-aware neighborhoods and drift-field objectives, achieving strong performance with low latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion-based trajectory planners offer rich multimodal action sequences but suffer from high inference-time costs due to iterative denoising, making them impractical for real-time closed-loop planning under tight compute budgets.

Method: Keyed Drifting Policies (KDP) use a conditioning-aware notion of neighborhood with drift-field objectives that attract generated trajectories toward condition-matched dataset windows while repelling from nearby generated samples, using stop-gradient drifted targets to amortize iterative refinement into training.

Result: KDP achieves strong performance across standard RL benchmarks and real-time hardware deployments with one-step inference and substantially lower planning latency than diffusion sampling.

Conclusion: KDP successfully enables diffusion-like trajectory planning behavior with single-step inference while retaining diversity and conditioning capabilities, making it suitable for real-time applications.

Abstract: Diffusion-based trajectory planners can synthesize rich, multimodal action sequences for offline reinforcement learning, but their iterative denoising incurs substantial inference-time cost, making closed-loop planning slow under tight compute budgets. We study the problem of achieving diffusion-like trajectory planning behavior with one-step inference, while retaining the ability to sample diverse candidate plans and condition on the current state in a receding-horizon control loop. Our key observation is that conditional trajectory generation fails under naïve distribution-matching objectives when the similarity measure used to align generated trajectories with the dataset is dominated by unconstrained future dimensions. In practice, this causes attraction toward average trajectories, collapses action diversity, and yields near-static behavior. Our key insight is that conditional generative planning requires a conditioning-aware notion of neighborhood: trajectory updates should be computed using distances in a compact key space that reflects the condition, while still applying updates in the full trajectory space. Building on this, we introduce Keyed Drifting Policies (KDP), a one-step trajectory generator trained with a drift-field objective that attracts generated trajectories toward condition-matched dataset windows and repels them from nearby generated samples, using a stop-gradient drifted target to amortize iterative refinement into training. At inference, the resulting policy produces a full trajectory window in a single forward pass. Across standard RL benchmarks and real-time hardware deployments, KDP achieves strong performance with one-step inference and substantially lower planning latency than diffusion sampling. Project website, code and videos: https://keyed-drifting.github.io/

</details>


### [146] [Stiffness Copilot: An Impedance Policy for Contact-Rich Teleoperation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14068)
*Yeping Wang,Zhengtong Xu,Pornthep Preechayasomboon,Ben Abbatematteo,Amirhossein H. Memar,Nick Colonnese,Sonny Chan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Stiffness Copilot is a vision-based policy for shared-control teleoperation that automatically adjusts robot impedance online while operators command pose, achieving both safety and efficiency through zero-shot transfer from simulation to real-world.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In contact-rich teleoperation tasks, selecting appropriate robot impedance is challenging - robots need to be compliant to avoid environmental damage but stiff for responsiveness and force application. Current approaches require manual stiffness selection which is difficult for operators.

Method: First infer direction-dependent stiffness matrices in simulation using privileged contact information, then use these matrices to supervise a lightweight vision policy that predicts robot stiffness from wrist-camera images. The policy transfers zero-shot to real images at runtime.

Result: In human-subject studies, Stiffness Copilot achieved safety comparable to using constant low stiffness while matching the efficiency of using constant high stiffness, demonstrating effective balance between safety and performance.

Conclusion: Stiffness Copilot enables effective shared-control teleoperation by automatically adjusting impedance based on visual input, solving the stiffness selection problem through simulation-trained vision policies that transfer to real-world applications.

Abstract: In teleoperation of contact-rich manipulation tasks, selecting robot impedance is critical but difficult. The robot must be compliant to avoid damaging the environment, but stiff to remain responsive and to apply force when needed. In this paper, we present Stiffness Copilot, a vision-based policy for shared-control teleoperation in which the operator commands robot pose and the policy adjusts robot impedance online. To train Stiffness Copilot, we first infer direction-dependent stiffness matrices in simulation using privileged contact information. We then use these matrices to supervise a lightweight vision policy that predicts robot stiffness from wrist-camera images and transfers zero-shot to real images at runtime. In a human-subject study, Stiffness Copilot achieved safety comparable to using a constant low stiffness while matching the efficiency of using a constant high stiffness.

</details>


### [147] [GelSphere: An Omnidirectional Rolling Vision-Based Tactile Sensor for Online 3D Reconstruction and Normal Force Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14104)
*Seoyeon Lee,Mohammad Amin Mirzaee,Wenzhen Yuan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: GelSphere is a spherical vision-based tactile sensor enabling omnidirectional rolling for continuous surface scanning with real-time reconstruction capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional vision-based tactile sensors have limitations: they can only sense locally, get damaged when slid across surfaces, and cylindrical sensors can only roll in fixed directions. There's a need for a sensor that can perform uninterrupted, omnidirectional surface scanning while maintaining sensing capabilities.

Method: The sensor uses a novel design with steel balls inside forming a bearing layer between the gel and rigid housing, enabling rolling motion in all axes. It streams tactile images via Wi-Fi and features online large-surface reconstruction capabilities.

Result: Quantitative results show the sensor maintains geometric fidelity and high reconstruction accuracy even under multi-directional rolling, enabling uninterrupted surface scanning with good image fusion performance.

Conclusion: GelSphere successfully addresses limitations of existing tactile sensors by enabling omnidirectional rolling for continuous surface scanning while maintaining sensing accuracy and geometric fidelity.

Abstract: We present GelSphere, a spherical vision-based tactile sensor designed for real-time continuous surface scanning. Unlike traditional vision-based tactile sensors that can only sense locally and are damaged when slid across surfaces, and cylindrical tactile sensors that can only roll along a fixed direction, our design enables omnidirectional rolling on surfaces. We accomplish this through our novel sensing system design, which has steel balls inside the sensor, forming a bearing layer between the gel and the rigid housing that allows rolling motion in all axes. The sensor streams tactile images through Wi-Fi, with online large-surface reconstruction capabilities. We present quantitative results for both reconstruction accuracy and image fusion performance. The results show that our sensor maintains geometric fidelity and high reconstruction accuracy even under multi-directional rolling, enabling uninterrupted surface scanning.

</details>


### [148] [H-RINS: Hierarchical Tightly-coupled Radar-Inertial Navigation via Smoothing and Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14109)
*Ali Alridha Abdulkarim,Mikhail Litvinov,Dzmitry Tsetserukou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A hierarchical radar-inertial factor graph framework that decouples estimation into high-rate resetting and persistent global graphs to address IMU bias observability challenges in radar-inertial odometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radar-inertial state estimation suffers from drift due to sparse radar measurements providing weak constraints on absolute orientation, making IMU biases poorly observable in typical sliding-window filters.

Method: Tightly coupled hierarchical factor graph with two components: (1) high-rate resetting graph fusing IMU preintegration, radar velocities, and adaptive ZUPT for real-time odometry; (2) persistent global graph maintaining full state with keyframe-based geometric mapping and loop closures using Incremental Smoothing and Mapping without explicit marginalization.

Result: System achieves high accuracy with drift-reduced estimation at 27x real-time execution speeds, demonstrating effective bias observability and drift mitigation.

Conclusion: The hierarchical framework successfully addresses fundamental observability challenges in radar-inertial odometry through probabilistic tight-coupling between persistent and resetting graphs, enabling continuous bias injection and drift reduction while maintaining real-time performance.

Abstract: Millimeter-wave radar provides robust perception in visually degraded environments. However, radar-inertial state estimation is inherently susceptible to drift. Because radar yields only sparse, body-frame velocity measurements, it provides weak constraints on absolute orientation. Consequently, IMU biases remain poorly observable over the short time horizons typical of sliding-window filters. To address this fundamental observability challenge, we propose a tightly coupled, hierarchical radar-inertial factor graph framework. Our architecture decouples the estimation problem into a high-rate resetting graph and a persistent global graph. The resetting graph fuses IMU preintegration, radar velocities, and adaptive Zero-Velocity Updates (ZUPT) to generate the smooth, low-latency odometry required for real-time control. Concurrently, the persistent graph is a full-state factor graph maintaining the complete information of poses, velocities, and biases by fusing inertial data with keyframe-based geometric mapping and loop closures. Leveraging Incremental Smoothing and Mapping, the persistent graph can operate without explicit marginalization of variables, preserving their information while ensuring long-term bias observability. The cornerstone of our approach is a probabilistic tight-coupling mechanism: fully observable, optimized biases and their exact covariances are continuously injected from the persistent graph into the resetting graph's prior, effectively anchoring the high-rate estimator against integration drift. Extensive evaluations demonstrate our system achieves high accuracy with drift-reduced estimation at 27x real-time execution speeds. We release the implementation code and datasets upon the acceptance of the paper.

</details>


### [149] [TransCurriculum: Multi-Dimensional Curriculum Learning for Fast & Stable Locomotion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14156)
*Prakhar Mishra,Amir Hossain Raj,Xuesu Xiao,Dinesh Manocha*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: TransCurriculum: Transformer-based multi-dimensional curriculum learning for agile quadrupedal locomotion that jointly adapts velocity commands, terrain difficulty, and domain randomization parameters, achieving higher speeds and reduced transfer losses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-speed legged locomotion suffers from stability issues and transfer losses at higher velocities due to single-axis curricula that ignore training history evolution. Existing approaches vary difficulty along single axes (velocity range, terrain difficulty, or domain parameters) using fixed rules or instantaneous rewards.

Method: Transformer-based teacher model retrieves sequence of rewards to predict future rewards, success rate, and learning progress, guiding expansion of 3D curriculum (velocity targets, terrain difficulty, friction/payload randomization). Reward history exploited at curriculum level rather than feeding directly into low-level policy.

Result: Achieves 6.3 m/s max velocity in simulation (Isaac Gym), outperforming prior curriculum baselines. Hardware deployment on Unitree Go1 achieves 4.1 m/s on carpet (18.8% faster than fastest curriculum methods). Reduces transfer loss to 18% from 27% for command-only curriculum, maintaining 80-90% success rate on rigid surfaces.

Conclusion: Multi-dimensional curriculum learning with transformer-based teacher effectively addresses stability and transfer loss issues in high-speed legged locomotion, demonstrating benefits of joint training across velocity, terrain, and domain randomization dimensions.

Abstract: High-speed legged locomotion struggles with stability and transfer losses at higher command velocities during deployment. One reason is that most curricula vary difficulty along single axis, for example increase the range of command velocities, terrain difficulty, or domain parameters (e.g. friction or payload mass) using either fixed update rule or instantaneous rewards while ignoring how the history of robot training has evolved. We propose TransCurriculum, a transformer-based multi-dimensional curriculum learning approach for agile quadrupedal locomotion. TransCurriculum adapts to 3 axes, velocity command targets, terrain difficulty, and domain randomization parameters (friction and payload mass). Rather than feeding task reward history directly into the low-level control policy, our formulation exploits it at the curriculum level. A transformer-based teacher retrieves the sequence of rewards and uses it to predict future rewards, success rate, and learning progress to guide expansion of this multidimensional curriculum towards high performing task bins. Finally we validate our approach on the Unitree Go1 robot in simulation (Isaac Gym) and deploy it zero-shot on Go1 hardware. Our TransCurriculum policy achieves a maximum velocity of 6.3 m/s in simulation and outperforms prior curriculum baselines. We tested our TransCurriculum trained policy on terrains (carpets, slopes, tiles, concrete), achieving a forward velocity of 4.1 m/s on carpet surpassing the fastest curriculum methods by 18.8% and achieves maximum zero-shot value among all tested methods. Our multi-dimensional curriculum also reduces the transfer loss to 18% from 27% for command only curriculum, demonstrating the benefits of joint training over velocity, terrain and domain randomization dimension while keeping the task success rate of 80-90% on rigid indoor and outdoor surfaces.

</details>


### [150] [See, Learn, Assist: Safe and Self-Paced Robotic Rehabilitation via Video-Based Learning from Demonstration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14160)
*Ali Alabbas,Camillo Murgia,Joanne Regan,Philip Long*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A framework for remote therapist-led robot-assisted rehabilitation using RGB-D video demonstrations encoded as 6-DoF trajectories with hybrid control for safety and adaptability across different rehabilitation modalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable therapists to remotely teach rehabilitation exercises via video demonstrations while ensuring accurate spatial generalization across diverse patient anatomies and maintaining safety during robot-assisted therapy.

Method: Uses RGB-D video to capture therapist demonstrations, encodes them as 6-DoF body-centric trajectories using Cartesian Dynamic Movement Primitives (DMPs). Implements decoupled hybrid control with spatially compliant virtual tunnel and effort-based temporal dilation. Supports three rehabilitation modalities (Passive, Active-Assisted, Active-Resistive) by linking execution phase to patient's tangential force. Employs Gaussian Mixture Regression (GMR) for on-the-fly abnormal force detection and trajectory reversal for safety.

Result: Achieved average trajectory reproduction error of 3.7cm and range of motion (ROM) error of 5.5 degrees. Dynamic interaction trials confirmed successful enforcement of effort-based progression while maintaining strict spatial path adherence against human disturbances.

Conclusion: The proposed framework enables effective remote therapist-led rehabilitation with accurate spatial generalization, adaptable control across therapy modalities, and robust safety mechanisms, demonstrating precision and reliability in experimental validation.

Abstract: In this paper, we propose a novel framework that allows therapists to teach robot-assisted rehabilitation exercises remotely via RGB-D video. Our system encodes demonstrations as 6-DoF body-centric trajectories using Cartesian Dynamic Movement Primitives (DMPs), ensuring accurate posture-independent spatial generalization across diverse patient anatomies. Crucially, we execute these trajectories through a decoupled hybrid control architecture that constructs a spatially compliant virtual tunnel, paired with an effort-based temporal dilation mechanism. This architecture is applied to three distinct rehabilitation modalities: Passive, Active-Assisted, and Active-Resistive, by dynamically linking the exercise's execution phase to the patient's tangential force contribution. To guarantee safety, a Gaussian Mixture Regression (GMR) model is learned on-the-fly from the patient's own limb. This allows the detection of abnormal interaction forces and, if necessary, reverses the trajectory to prevent injury. Experimental validation demonstrates the system's precision, achieving an average trajectory reproduction error of 3.7cm and a range of motion (ROM) error of 5.5 degrees. Furthermore, dynamic interaction trials confirm that the controller successfully enforces effort-based progression while maintaining strict spatial path adherence against human disturbances.

</details>


### [151] [Towards Equitable Robotic Furnishing Agents for Aging-in-Place: ADL-Grounded Design Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14182)
*Hansoo Lee,Changhee Seo,Subin Park,Sonya S. Kwak*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper argues for shifting robotics for older adults from convenience features to equity-focused systems, proposes a robotic furnishing-agent concept using AI/LLMs for ADL support, and validates requirements through user interviews.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current robotics for aging-in-place often focus on convenience rather than addressing the compounded cognitive-physical burden from accumulated small ADL difficulties, which affect wellbeing through fatigue, anxiety, reduced autonomy, and safety risks. There's a need to center equity, justice, and responsibility in robotic wellbeing systems.

Method: 1) ADL-grounded semi-structured interviews with four adults (70s-80s) to identify recurrent challenges and derive requirements; 2) Proposed robotic furnishing-agent concept using computer vision, generative AI, and LLMs for natural-language interaction, context-aware reminders, safe actuation, and transparency; 3) Video-stimulated follow-up interviews with same participants to validate preferences and identify equity concerns.

Result: Identified key ADL challenges: finding/organizing items, medication management, and object transport. Derived requirements for reducing cognitive-physical burden. User preferences included: confirmation before actuation, predictability, adjustable speed/autonomy, multimodal feedback. Also identified equity-related concerns about robotic deployment.

Conclusion: Robotics for older adult wellbeing must prioritize equity, justice, and responsibility over mere convenience. The proposed AI-enhanced robotic furnishing-agent concept addresses compounded ADL burdens, but open questions remain about evaluating and deploying equitable systems in real homes.

Abstract: In aging-in-place contexts, small difficulties in Activities of Daily Living (ADL) can accumulate, affecting well-being through fatigue, anxiety, reduced autonomy, and safety risks. This position paper argues that robotics for older adult wellbeing must move beyond "convenience features" and centre equity, justice, and responsibility. We conducted ADL-grounded semi-structured interviews with four adults in their 70s-80s, identifying recurrent challenges (finding/ organising items, taking medication, and transporting objects) and deriving requirements to reduce compounded cognitive-physical burden. Based on these insights, we propose an in-home robotic furnishing-agent concept leveraging computer vision and generative AI and LLMs for natural-language interaction, context-aware reminders, safe actuation, and user-centred transparency. We then report video-stimulated follow-up interviews with the same participants, highlighting preferences for confirmation before actuation, predictability, adjustable speed/autonomy, and multimodal feedback, as well as equity-related concerns. We conclude with open questions on evaluating and deploying equitable robotic wellbeing systems in real homes.

</details>


### [152] [Navigation beyond Wayfinding: Robots Collaborating with Visually Impaired Users for Environmental Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14216)
*Shaojun Cai,Nuwan Janaka,Ashwin Ram,Janidu Shehan,Yingjia Wan,Kotaro Hara,David Hsu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Robotic guide dog system enables BVI users to navigate through environments requiring object manipulation by alternating between lead mode (robot guides to target) and adaptation mode (robot adjusts to user's physical interactions).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing robotic guidance systems for blind and visually impaired individuals focus on clear path navigation but lack support for environmental interactions requiring object manipulation, which is critical for realistic navigation scenarios.

Method: Collaborative human-robot approach combining robotic guide dog's precise sensing/localization with user's physical manipulation capabilities, alternating between lead mode (robot detects and guides to target) and adaptation mode (robot adjusts motion during user interactions).

Result: System enables safer, smoother, and more efficient navigation than traditional white cane and non-adaptive guiding systems, with performance gap increasing for tasks requiring higher precision in locating interaction targets.

Conclusion: Human-robot collaboration shows promise for advancing assistive technologies toward more generalizable and realistic navigation support by addressing both wayfinding and environmental interaction challenges.

Abstract: Robotic guidance systems have shown promise in supporting blind and visually impaired (BVI) individuals with wayfinding and obstacle avoidance. However, most existing systems assume a clear path and do not support a critical aspect of navigation - environmental interactions that require manipulating objects to enable movement. These interactions are challenging for a human-robot pair because they demand (i) precise localization and manipulation of interaction targets (e.g., pressing elevator buttons) and (ii) dynamic coordination between the user's and robot's movements (e.g., pulling out a chair to sit). We present a collaborative human-robot approach that combines our robotic guide dog's precise sensing and localization capabilities with the user's ability to perform physical manipulation. The system alternates between two modes: lead mode, where the robot detects and guides the user to the target, and adaptation mode, where the robot adjusts its motion as the user interacts with the environment (e.g., opening a door). Evaluation results show that our system enables navigation that is safer, smoother, and more efficient than both a traditional white cane and a non-adaptive guiding system, with the performance gap widening as tasks demand higher precision in locating interaction targets. These findings highlight the promise of human-robot collaboration in advancing assistive technologies toward more generalizable and realistic navigation support.

</details>


### [153] [A Real-Time Neuro-Symbolic Ethical Governor for Safe Decision Control in Autonomous Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14221)
*Aueaphum Aueawatthanaphisut,Kuepon Aueawatthanaphisut*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Real-time neuro-symbolic ethical governor for autonomous robots integrates transformer-based ethical reasoning with probabilistic risk fields and override control, validated in simulated manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ethical decision governance is critical for autonomous robotic systems operating in human-centered and safety-sensitive environments, requiring real-time supervisory control mechanisms.

Method: Neuro-symbolic framework combining transformer-based ethical reasoning (fine-tuned DistilBERT on ETHICS dataset) with probabilistic ethical risk field formulation and threshold-based override control mechanism.

Result: Experimental validation shows stable model convergence, reliable ethical risk discrimination, improved safety-aware decisions without significant task efficiency degradation, and enhanced interpretability.

Conclusion: Ethical decision governance can be effectively modeled as a dynamic supervisory risk layer for autonomous robotic systems, with broader applicability to cyber-physical and assistive robotics domains.

Abstract: Ethical decision governance has become a critical requirement for autonomous robotic systems operating in human-centered and safety-sensitive environments. This paper presents a real-time neuro-symbolic ethical governor designed to enable risk-aware supervisory control in autonomous robotic manipulation tasks. The proposed framework integrates transformer-based ethical reasoning with a probabilistic ethical risk field formulation and a threshold-based override control mechanism. language-grounded ethical intent inference capability is learned from natural language task descriptions using a fine-tuned DistilBERT model trained on the ETHICS commonsense dataset. A continuous ethical risk metric is subsequently derived from predicted unsafe action probability, confidence uncertainty, and probabilistic variance to support adaptive decision filtering. The effectiveness of the proposed approach is validated through simulated autonomous robot-arm task scenarios involving varying levels of human proximity and operational hazard. Experimental results demonstrate stable model convergence, reliable ethical risk discrimination, and improved safety-aware decision outcomes without significant degradation of task execution efficiency. The proposed neuro-symbolic architecture further provides enhanced interpretability compared with purely data-driven safety filters, enabling transparent ethical reasoning in real-time control loops. The findings suggest that ethical decision governance can be effectively modeled as a dynamic supervisory risk layer for autonomous robotic systems, with potential applicability to broader cyber-physical and assistive robotics domains.

</details>


### [154] [AeroGen: Agentic Drone Autonomy through Single-Shot Structured Prompting & Drone SDK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14236)
*Kautuk Astu,Yogesh Simmhan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: AeroGen is an open-loop framework that enables consistently correct single-shot AI-generated drone control programs through structured guardrail prompting integrated with the AeroDaaS drone SDK.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Designing correct UAV autonomy programs is challenging due to joint navigation, sensing and analytics requirements. While LLMs can generate code, their reliability for safety-critical UAVs remains uncertain.

Method: AeroGen encodes API descriptions, flight constraints and operational world rules directly into the system context prompt, enabling generic LLMs to produce constraint-aware code from user prompts with minimal example code. It uses structured guardrail prompting integrated with the AeroDaaS drone SDK.

Result: AeroGen generates about 40 lines of AeroDaaS Python code in about 20s per mission across 20 navigation tasks and 5 drone missions in urban, farm and inspection environments. It works in both real-world and simulations, showing improved robustness, correctness and deployability.

Conclusion: Structured prompting with a well-defined SDK improves robustness, correctness and deployability of LLM-generated drone autonomy programs, enabling consistently correct single-shot AI-generated drone control.

Abstract: Designing correct UAV autonomy programs is challenging due to joint navigation, sensing and analytics requirements. While LLMs can generate code, their reliability for safety-critical UAVs remains uncertain. This paper presents AeroGen, an open-loop framework that enables consistently correct single-shot AI-generated drone control programs through structured guardrail prompting and integration with the AeroDaaS drone SDK. AeroGen encodes API descriptions, flight constraints and operational world rules directly into the system context prompt, enabling generic LLMs to produce constraint-aware code from user prompts, with minimal example code. We evaluate AeroGen across a diverse benchmark of 20 navigation tasks and 5 drone missions on urban, farm and inspection environments, using both imperative and declarative user prompts. AeroGen generates about 40 lines of AeroDaaS Python code in about 20s per mission, in both real-world and simulations, showing that structured prompting with a well-defined SDK improves robustness, correctness and deployability of LLM-generated drone autonomy programs.

</details>


### [155] [Design of a Bio-Inspired Miniature Submarine for Low-Cost Water Quality Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14244)
*Quang Huy Vu,Quan Le,Manh Duong Phung*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A bio-inspired miniature submarine using squid-like jet propulsion for low-cost water quality monitoring, achieving stable navigation and depth control with $122.5 hardware cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Water quality monitoring is essential for protecting aquatic ecosystems and detecting pollution, but existing solutions may be expensive or complex. There's a need for affordable, compact platforms suitable for educational and environmental monitoring applications.

Method: The system employs bio-inspired design with squid-like jet propulsion using pump-driven water jets for propulsion/steering, and pump-based buoyancy control for depth regulation and water sampling. It integrates low-cost commercial components (ESP32, IMU, pressure sensor, GPS, LoRa) and was validated through pool tests and lake field trials.

Result: The complete system costs approximately $122.5. Experimental results show: stable attitude control with roll/pitch deviations within ±2°/±1.5° during 360° rotation; heading step response with ~2s rise time and 5s settling time; depth control achieving 2.5m target with ±0.1m steady-state error; and successful field navigation and water sampling operations.

Conclusion: The proposed platform provides a compact, stable, and cost-effective solution for small-scale aquatic environmental monitoring, demonstrating practical viability for educational and environmental applications through successful experimental validation.

Abstract: Water quality monitoring is essential for protecting aquatic ecosystems and detecting environmental pollution. This paper presents the design and experimental validation of a bio-inspired miniature submarine for low-cost water quality monitoring. Inspired by the jet propulsion mechanism of squids, the proposed system employs pump-driven water jets for propulsion and steering, combined with a pump-based buoyancy control mechanism that enables both depth regulation and water sampling. The vehicle integrates low-cost, commercially available components including an ESP32 microcontroller, IMU, pressure sensor, GPS receiver, and LoRa communication module. The complete system can be constructed at a hardware cost of approximately $122.5, making it suitable for educational and environmental monitoring applications. Experimental validation was conducted through pool tests and field trials in a lake. During a 360 degrees rotation test, roll and pitch deviations remained within +/-2 degrees and +/-1.5 degrees, respectively, demonstrating stable attitude control. Steering experiments showed a heading step response with approximately 2 s rise time and 5 s settling time. Depth control experiments achieved a target depth of 2.5 m with steady-state error within +/-0.1 m. Field experiments further demonstrated reliable navigation and successful water sampling operations. The results confirm that the proposed platform provides a compact, stable, and cost-effective solution for small-scale aquatic environmental monitoring.

</details>


### [156] [Load-Aware Locomotion Control for Humanoid Robots in Industrial Transportation Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14308)
*Lequn Fu,Yijun Zhong,Xiao Li,Yibin Liu,Zhiyuan Xu,Jian Tang,Shiqi Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A load-aware locomotion framework for industrial humanoid robots that enables stable loco-manipulation under varying payloads through reinforcement learning with kinematic guidance and disturbance encoding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Industrial humanoids need to perform load-carrying tasks that couple locomotion and manipulation, but achieving stable locomotion under varying payloads and upper-body motions is challenging due to dynamic coupling and partial observability.

Method: Decoupled yet coordinated loco-manipulation architecture with RL policy for lower-body locomotion producing residual joint actions on kinematically derived nominal configurations. Uses kinematics-based locomotion reference with height-conditioned joint-space offset, and history-based state estimator that infers base velocity/height and encodes disturbances in latent representation.

Result: Framework trained entirely in simulation and deployed on full-size humanoid without fine-tuning. Experiments demonstrate faster training, accurate height tracking, and stable loco-manipulation in both simulation and real-world settings.

Conclusion: The proposed load-aware locomotion framework effectively addresses the challenges of dynamic coupling and partial observability in industrial humanoid loco-manipulation tasks, enabling robust performance under varying payload conditions.

Abstract: Humanoid robots deployed in industrial environments are required to perform load-carrying transportation tasks that tightly couple locomotion and manipulation. However, achieving stable and robust locomotion under varying payloads and upper-body motions is challenging due to dynamic coupling and partial observability. This paper presents a load-aware locomotion framework for industrial humanoids based on a decoupled yet coordinated loco-manipulation architecture. Lower-body locomotion is controlled via a reinforcement learning policy producing residual joint actions on kinematically derived nominal configurations. A kinematics-based locomotion reference with a height-conditioned joint-space offset guides learning, while a history-based state estimator infers base linear velocity and height and encodes residual load- and manipulation-induced disturbances in a compact latent representation. The framework is trained entirely in simulation and deployed on a full-size humanoid robot without fine-tuning. Simulation and real-world experiments demonstrate faster training, accurate height tracking, and stable loco-manipulation. Project page: https://lequn-f.github.io/LALO/

</details>


### [157] [OmniClone: Engineering a Robust, All-Rounder Whole-Body Humanoid Teleoperation System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14327)
*Yixuan Li,Le Ma,Yutang Lin,Yushi Du,Mengya Liu,Kaizhe Hu,Jieming Cui,Yixin Zhu,Wei Liang,Baoxiong Jia,Siyuan Huang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OmniClone is a whole-body humanoid teleoperation system that achieves high-fidelity control with minimal computational resources, featuring diagnostic benchmarking and a unified policy supporting multiple control sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing teleoperation systems lack diagnostic granularity, use aggregate metrics that mask failure modes, have tightly coupled configurations, and are computationally expensive, hindering robust real-world deployment.

Method: Developed OmniBench diagnostic benchmark for stratified motion evaluation, optimized training data recipe, subject-agnostic retargeting, robust communication, and a unified control-source-agnostic policy supporting teleoperation, motion playback, and VLA models.

Result: Reduced Mean Per-Joint Position Error (MPJPE) by over 66%, requires orders-of-magnitude fewer computational resources (single consumer GPU), generalizes across operators with different body proportions, and supports multiple control sources.

Conclusion: OmniClone provides an accessible, robust foundation for scalable humanoid teleoperation and autonomous learning by uniting diagnostic evaluation with practical engineering improvements.

Abstract: Whole-body humanoid teleoperation enables humans to remotely control humanoid robots, serving as both a real-time operational tool and a scalable engine for collecting demonstrations for autonomous learning. Despite recent advances, existing systems are validated using aggregate metrics that conflate distinct motion regimes, masking critical failure modes. This lack of diagnostic granularity, compounded by tightly coupled and labor-intensive system configurations, hinders robust real-world deployment. A key open challenge is building a teleoperation system that is simultaneously robust, versatile, and affordable for practical use. Here we present OmniClone, a whole-body humanoid teleoperation system that achieves high-fidelity, multi-skill control on a single consumer GPU with modest data requirements. Central to our approach is OmniBench, a diagnostic benchmark that evaluates policies across stratified motion categories and difficulty levels on unseen motions, exposing the narrow specialization of prior systems. Guided by these diagnostics, we identify an optimized training data recipe and integrate system-level improvements: subject-agnostic retargeting and robust communication, that collectively reduce Mean Per-Joint Position Error (MPJPE) by over 66% while requiring orders-of-magnitude fewer computational resources than comparable methods. Crucially, OmniClone is control-source-agnostic: a single unified policy supports real-time teleoperation, generated motion playback, and Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, while generalizing across operators of vastly different body proportions. By uniting diagnostic evaluation with practical engineering, OmniClone provides an accessible foundation for scalable humanoid teleoperation and autonomous learning.

</details>


### [158] [Data-Driven Physics Embedded Dynamics with Predictive Control and Reinforcement Learning for Quadrupeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14333)
*Prakrut Kotecha,Aditya Shirwatkar,Shishir Kolathaya*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Integrating Lagrangian Neural Networks (LNNs) into RL-MPC framework improves physical consistency, reduces long-horizon errors, and achieves 4x computational efficiency while maintaining task performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current RL-MPC approaches for quadrupedal locomotion suffer from compounding errors over long horizons and limited interpretability due to lack of physical inductive biases, requiring more physically consistent dynamics learning.

Method: Integrates Lagrangian Neural Networks (LNNs) into RL-MPC framework for physically consistent dynamics learning, using inverse dynamics infinite horizon MPC scheme that avoids costly matrix inversions.

Result: Achieves 4x computational efficiency improvement with minimal task performance loss, shows improved sample efficiency, reduced long-horizon error, faster real-time planning compared to unstructured neural dynamics, and validates on Unitree Go1 robot.
