Conclusion: LNN integration into RL-MPC framework provides physically consistent dynamics learning, addresses compounding errors and interpretability issues, enables efficient real-time planning, and demonstrates real-world viability on quadrupedal robots.

Abstract: State of the art quadrupedal locomotion approaches integrate Model Predictive Control (MPC) with Reinforcement Learning (RL), enabling complex motion capabilities with planning and terrain adaptive behaviors. However, they often face compounding errors over long horizons and have limited interpretability due to the absence of physical inductive biases. We address these issues by integrating Lagrangian Neural Networks (LNNs) into an RL MPC framework, enabling physically consistent dynamics learning. At deployment, our inverse dynamics infinite horizon MPC scheme avoids costly matrix inversions, improving computational efficiency by up to 4x with minimal loss of task performance. We validate our framework through multiple ablations of the proposed LNN and its variants. We show improved sample efficiency, reduced long-horizon error, and faster real time planning compared to unstructured neural dynamics. Lastly, we also test our framework on the Unitree Go1 robot to show real world viability.

</details>


### [159] [VIP-Loco: A Visually Guided Infinite Horizon Planning Framework for Legged Locomotion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14345)
*Aditya Shirwatkar,Satyam Gupta,Shishir Kolathaya*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: VIP-Loco integrates vision-based scene understanding with RL and MPC for robust legged robot locomotion across diverse challenging terrains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridging the gap between MPC's interpretable planning but poor handling of high-dimensional perceptual inputs, and RL's adaptability to visual challenges but lack of planning, for robust perceptive locomotion.

Method: During training: internal model maps proprioceptive states and depth images into compact kinodynamic features for RL policy. At deployment: learned models used within infinite-horizon MPC formulation combining adaptability with structured planning.

Result: Validated in simulation on challenging locomotion tasks (slopes, stairs, crawling, tilting, gap jumping, climbing) across three robot morphologies (quadruped, biped, wheeled-biped), showing robust performance through ablations and comparisons with SOTA methods.

Conclusion: VIP-Loco successfully unifies planning and perception, enabling robust, interpretable locomotion in diverse environments by combining RL's adaptability with MPC's structured planning.

Abstract: Perceptive locomotion for legged robots requires anticipating and adapting to complex, dynamic environments. Model Predictive Control (MPC) serves as a strong baseline, providing interpretable motion planning with constraint enforcement, but struggles with high-dimensional perceptual inputs and rapidly changing terrain. In contrast, model-free Reinforcement Learning (RL) adapts well across visually challenging scenarios but lacks planning. To bridge this gap, we propose VIP-Loco, a framework that integrates vision-based scene understanding with RL and planning. During training, an internal model maps proprioceptive states and depth images into compact kinodynamic features used by the RL policy. At deployment, the learned models are used within an infinite-horizon MPC formulation, combining adaptability with structured planning. We validate VIP-Loco in simulation on challenging locomotion tasks, including slopes, stairs, crawling, tilting, gap jumping, and climbing, across three robot morphologies: a quadruped (Unitree Go1), a biped (Cassie), and a wheeled-biped (TronA1-W). Through ablations and comparisons with state-of-the-art methods, we show that VIP-Loco unifies planning and perception, enabling robust, interpretable locomotion in diverse environments.

</details>


### [160] [OxyGen: Unified KV Cache Management for Vision-Language-Action Models under Multi-Task Parallelism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14371)
*Xiangyu Li,Huaizhi Tang,Xin Ding,Weijun Wang,Ting Cao,Yunxin Liu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OxyGen introduces unified KV cache management for Mixture-of-Transformers VLAs to enable efficient parallel execution of heterogeneous tasks (language, action, memory) by eliminating redundant computation and resource contention through cross-task KV sharing and cross-frame continuous batching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Embodied AI agents require parallel execution of multiple tasks (manipulation, conversation, memory construction) from shared observations with distinct time constraints. While Mixture-of-Transformers VLAs architecturally support heterogeneous outputs, existing inference systems fail to achieve efficient multi-task parallelism for on-device deployment due to redundant computation and resource contention caused by isolated KV cache management.

Method: Proposes unified KV cache management as an inference paradigm that treats KV cache as a first-class shared resource across tasks and over time. This enables two key optimizations: 1) cross-task KV sharing eliminates redundant prefill of shared observations, and 2) cross-frame continuous batching decouples variable-length language decoding from fixed-rate action generation across control cycles. Implemented for π₀.₅, the most popular MoT VLA.

Result: OxyGen achieves up to 3.7× speedup over isolated execution, delivering over 200 tokens/s language throughput and 70 Hz action frequency simultaneously without action quality degradation. Evaluated under representative robotic configurations.

Conclusion: Unified KV cache management effectively addresses the inefficiencies of isolated KV cache management in MoT VLAs, enabling efficient parallel execution of heterogeneous tasks for embodied AI agents through cross-task sharing and temporal decoupling optimizations.

Abstract: Embodied AI agents increasingly require parallel execution of multiple tasks, such as manipulation, conversation, and memory construction, from shared observations under distinct time constraints. Recent Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) Vision-Language-Action Models (VLAs) architecturally support such heterogeneous outputs, yet existing inference systems fail to achieve efficient multi-task parallelism for on-device deployment due to redundant computation and resource contention. We identify isolated KV cache management as the root cause. To address this, we propose unified KV cache management, an inference paradigm that treats KV cache as a first-class shared resource across tasks and over time. This abstraction enables two key optimizations: cross-task KV sharing eliminates redundant prefill of shared observations, while cross-frame continuous batching decouples variable-length language decoding from fixed-rate action generation across control cycles. We implement this paradigm for $π_{0.5}$, the most popular MoT VLA, and evaluate under representative robotic configurations. OxyGen achieves up to 3.7$\times$ speedup over isolated execution, delivering over 200 tokens/s language throughput and 70 Hz action frequency simultaneously without action quality degradation.

</details>


### [161] [From Scanning Guidelines to Action: A Robotic Ultrasound Agent with LLM-Based Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14393)
*Yuan Bi,Yiping Zhou,Pei Liu,Feng Li,Zhongliang Jiang,Nassir Navab*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LLM-based agent framework for autonomous robotic ultrasound scanning that interprets guidelines and dynamically adapts scanning procedures using software tools, with RL fine-tuning for improved reasoning and tool selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotic ultrasound offers reproducibility benefits but current systems rely on fixed procedures that are difficult to adapt to new scanning tasks. Human sonographer expertise involves complex decision-making based on experience and situational judgment that needs to be transferred to robotic systems.

Method: Proposed unified framework using LLM-based agent to interpret US scanning guidelines and execute scans by dynamically invoking software tools. The agent retrieves and reasons over guideline steps from handbooks, adapting planning based on observations and current scanning state. RL-based fine-tuning strategy improves reasoning quality and correctness of tool selection/parameterization while maintaining generalization.

Result: Validated via verbal execution on 10 US scanning guidelines, showing improved reasoning and tool selection with RL fine-tuning. Demonstrated real-world feasibility on robotic scanning of gallbladder, spine, and kidney. Framework successfully follows diverse guidelines and enables reliable autonomous scanning across multiple anatomical targets.

Conclusion: The LLM-based agent framework enables adaptive, guideline-driven robotic ultrasound scanning that handles variable workflows and decision-dependent processes, providing a unified system for autonomous scanning across multiple anatomical targets with transparent reasoning.

Abstract: Robotic ultrasound offers advantages over free-hand scanning, including improved reproducibility and reduced operator dependency. In clinical practice, US acquisition relies heavily on the sonographer's experience and situational judgment. When transferring this process to robotic systems, such expertise is often encoded explicitly through fixed procedures and task-specific models, yielding pipelines that can be difficult to adapt to new scanning tasks. In this work, we propose a unified framework for autonomous robotic US scanning that leverages a LLM-based agent to interpret US scanning guidelines and execute scans by dynamically invoking a set of provided software tools. Instead of encoding fixed scanning procedures, the LLM agent retrieves and reasons over guideline steps from scanning handbooks and adapts its planning decisions based on observations and the current scanning state. This enables the system to handle variable and decision-dependent workflows, such as adjusting scanning strategies, repeating steps, or selecting the appropriate next tool call in response to image quality or anatomical findings. Because the reasoning underlying tool selection is also critical for transparent and trustworthy planning, we further fine tune the LLM agent using a RL based strategy to improve both its reasoning quality and the correctness of tool selection and parameterization, while maintaining robust generalization to unseen guidelines and related tasks. We first validate the approach via verbal execution on 10 US scanning guidelines, assessing reasoning as well as tool selection and parameterization, and showing the benefit of RL fine tuning. We then demonstrate real world feasibility on robotic scanning of the gallbladder, spine, and kidney. Overall, the framework follows diverse guidelines and enables reliable autonomous scanning across multiple anatomical targets within a unified system.

</details>


### [162] [eNavi: Event-based Imitation Policies for Low-Light Indoor Mobile Robot Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14397)
*Prithvi Jai Ramesh,Kaustav Chanda,Krishna Vinod,Joseph Raj Vishal,Yezhou Yang,Bharatesh Chakravarthi*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A real-world indoor person-following dataset with synchronized event streams, RGB frames, and control actions is introduced, along with a multimodal preprocessing pipeline and a late-fusion RGB-Event navigation policy that outperforms RGB-only models, especially in low-light conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Event cameras offer high dynamic range and microsecond temporal resolution advantages for indoor robot navigation, but there's limited research on end-to-end control policies that exploit asynchronous event streams. The paper aims to address this gap by creating a dataset and developing event-based navigation policies.

Method: 1) Collected a real-world indoor person-following dataset using TurtleBot 2 with synchronized raw event streams, RGB frames, and expert control actions across multiple indoor maps and conditions. 2) Built a multimodal data preprocessing pipeline for temporal alignment and ground-truth action reconstruction. 3) Proposed a late-fusion RGB-Event navigation policy combining dual MobileNet encoders with a transformer-based fusion module trained via behavioral cloning.

Result: Systematic evaluation across 12 training variations shows that policies incorporating event data, particularly the RGB-Event fusion model, achieve improved robustness and lower action prediction error compared to RGB-only models. The fusion model performs especially well in unseen low-light conditions where RGB-only models fail.

Conclusion: Event-based perception enhances robot navigation robustness, particularly in challenging lighting conditions. The released dataset, synchronization pipeline, and trained models provide resources for advancing event-based navigation research.

Abstract: Event cameras provide high dynamic range and microsecond-level temporal resolution, making them well-suited for indoor robot navigation, where conventional RGB cameras degrade under fast motion or low-light conditions. Despite advances in event-based perception spanning detection, SLAM, and pose estimation, there remains limited research on end-to-end control policies that exploit the asynchronous nature of event streams. To address this gap, we introduce a real-world indoor person-following dataset collected using a TurtleBot 2 robot, featuring synchronized raw event streams, RGB frames, and expert control actions across multiple indoor maps, trajectories under both normal and low-light conditions. We further build a multimodal data preprocessing pipeline that temporally aligns event and RGB observations while reconstructing ground-truth actions from odometry to support high-quality imitation learning. Building on this dataset, we propose a late-fusion RGB-Event navigation policy that combines dual MobileNet encoders with a transformer-based fusion module trained via behavioral cloning. A systematic evaluation of RGB-only, Event-only, and RGB-Event fusion models across 12 training variations ranging from single-path imitation to general multi-path imitation shows that policies incorporating event data, particularly the fusion model, achieve improved robustness and lower action prediction error, especially in unseen low-light conditions where RGB-only models fail. We release the dataset, synchronization pipeline, and trained models at https://eventbasedvision.github.io/eNavi/

</details>


### [163] [OCRA: Object-Centric Learning with 3D and Tactile Priors for Human-to-Robot Action Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14401)
*Kuanning Wang,Ke Fan,Yuqian Fu,Siyu Lin,Hu Luo,Daniel Seita,Yanwei Fu,Yu-Gang Jiang,Xiangyang Xue*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OCRA is an object-centric framework that learns manipulation skills directly from human demonstration videos by combining 3D visual reconstruction with tactile priors for robust action transfer to robots.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable robots to learn manipulation skills directly from human demonstration videos in a scalable and robust manner, addressing the limitations of vision-only approaches by incorporating tactile information for properties not easily perceived visually.

Method: Uses multi-view RGB videos with VGGT 3D foundation model and detection/segmentation models to reconstruct object-centric 3D point clouds, incorporates tactile priors from over 1M tactile images via multimodal ResFiLM fusion, and generates actions with Diffusion Policy.

Result: OCRA significantly outperforms existing baselines and ablations on both vision-only and visuo-tactile tasks, demonstrating effectiveness for learning from human demonstration videos.

Conclusion: The object-centric approach combined with multimodal fusion of 3D visual reconstruction and tactile priors enables robust manipulation learning directly from human demonstration videos, providing a scalable framework for human-to-robot action transfer.

Abstract: We present OCRA, an Object-Centric framework for video-based human-to-Robot Action transfer that learns directly from human demonstration videos to enable robust manipulation. Object-centric learning emphasizes task-relevant objects and their interactions while filtering out irrelevant background, providing a natural and scalable way to teach robots. OCRA leverages multi-view RGB videos, the state-of-the-art 3D foundation model VGGT, and advanced detection and segmentation models to reconstruct object-centric 3D point clouds, capturing rich interactions between objects. To handle properties not easily perceived by vision alone, we incorporate tactile priors via a large-scale dataset of over one million tactile images. These 3D and tactile priors are fused through a multimodal module (ResFiLM) and fed into a Diffusion Policy to generate robust manipulation actions. Extensive experiments on both vision-only and visuo-tactile tasks show that OCRA significantly outperforms existing baselines and ablations, demonstrating its effectiveness for learning from human demonstration videos.

</details>


### [164] [Towards Versatile Opti-Acoustic Sensor Fusion and Volumetric Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14457)
*Ivana Collado-Gonzalez,John McConnell,Brendan Englot*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A volumetric mapping framework fuses stereo sonar with monocular camera to enable safe underwater navigation in varying visibility conditions by resolving sonar elevation ambiguity and combining complementary sensor strengths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Underwater vehicles need accurate 3D mapping for obstacle-rich environments. Vision fails in turbid conditions, while sonar has low resolution and elevation ambiguity. A fusion approach is needed to leverage complementary strengths of both sensors for reliable navigation across varying visibility conditions.

Method: Framework fuses stereo sonar pair with monocular camera. Overlapping sonar fields resolve elevation ambiguity to produce 3D point clouds. Camera identifies regions of interest, associates with sonar returns, and combines sonar range with camera-derived elevation cues to generate additional 3D points. Each point gets confidence value reflecting reliability. Confidence-weighted points are fused using Gaussian Process Volumetric Mapping that prioritizes most reliable measurements.

Result: Experimental comparisons with other opti-acoustic and sonar-based approaches, plus field tests in marina environment, demonstrate method's effectiveness in capturing complex geometries and preserving critical navigation information in both clear and turbid conditions. Code is open-source for community adoption.

Conclusion: The proposed sensor fusion framework successfully addresses limitations of individual underwater perception modalities by combining stereo sonar's robustness to turbidity with camera's high resolution, enabling reliable 3D volumetric mapping for safe navigation across varying visibility conditions.

Abstract: Accurate 3D volumetric mapping is critical for autonomous underwater vehicles operating in obstacle-rich environments. Vision-based perception provides high-resolution data but fails in turbid conditions, while sonar is robust to lighting and turbidity but suffers from low resolution and elevation ambiguity. This paper presents a volumetric mapping framework that fuses a stereo sonar pair with a monocular camera to enable safe navigation under varying visibility conditions. Overlapping sonar fields of view resolve elevation ambiguity, producing fully defined 3D point clouds at each time step. The framework identifies regions of interest in camera images, associates them with corresponding sonar returns, and combines sonar range with camera-derived elevation cues to generate additional 3D points. Each 3D point is assigned a confidence value reflecting its reliability. These confidence-weighted points are fused using a Gaussian Process Volumetric Mapping framework that prioritizes the most reliable measurements. Experimental comparisons with other opti-acoustic and sonar-based approaches, along with field tests in a marina environment, demonstrate the method's effectiveness in capturing complex geometries and preserving critical information for robot navigation in both clear and turbid conditions. Our code is open-source to support community adoption.

</details>


### [165] [Physics-Informed Policy Optimization via Analytic Dynamics Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14469)
*Namai Chandra,Liu Mohan,Zhihao Gu,Lin Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PIPER introduces a physics-informed RL framework that integrates analytical soft physics constraints into neural policy optimization to improve learning efficiency and physical consistency in robotic control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current RL methods for robotic control suffer from high sample complexity and produce physically inconsistent actions, despite accurate dynamics models being readily available in simulators. Neural policies implicitly rediscover complex physics from data alone, which is inefficient.

Method: PIPER integrates physical constraints directly into neural policy optimization using analytical soft physics constraints. The core innovation is adding a differentiable Lagrangian residual as a regularization term within the actor's objective. This residual, extracted from the robot's simulator description, biases policy updates toward dynamically consistent solutions without requiring changes to existing simulators or core RL algorithms.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that PIPER significantly improves learning efficiency, stability, and control accuracy compared to state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: PIPER establishes a new paradigm for efficient and physically consistent robotic control by seamlessly integrating physical constraints into RL policy optimization through analytical soft constraints.

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) has achieved strong performance in robotic control; however, state-of-the-art policy learning methods, such as actor-critic methods, still suffer from high sample complexity and often produce physically inconsistent actions. This limitation stems from neural policies implicitly rediscovering complex physics from data alone, despite accurate dynamics models being readily available in simulators. In this paper, we introduce a novel physics-informed RL framework, called PIPER, that seamlessly integrates physical constraints directly into neural policy optimization with analytical soft physics constraints. At the core of our method is the integration of a differentiable Lagrangian residual as a regularization term within the actor's objective. This residual, extracted from a robot's simulator description, subtly biases policy updates towards dynamically consistent solutions. Crucially, this physics integration is realized through an additional loss term during policy optimization, requiring no alterations to existing simulators or core RL algorithms. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method significantly improves learning efficiency, stability, and control accuracy, establishing a new paradigm for efficient and physically consistent robotic control.

</details>


### [166] [R3DP: Real-Time 3D-Aware Policy for Embodied Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14498)
*Yuhao Zhang,Wanxi Dong,Yue Shi,Yi Liang,Jingnan Gao,Qiaochu Yang,Yaxing Lyu,Zhixuan Liang,Yibin Liu,Congsheng Xu,Xianda Guo,Wei Sui,Yaohui Jin,Xiaokang Yang,Yanyan Xu,Yao Mu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: R3DP integrates powerful 3D priors into real-time manipulation policies via asynchronous fast-slow collaboration, achieving superior performance with reduced latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale 3D vision models provide strong priors for embodied manipulation but incur prohibitive computational latency for real-time control, creating a need to integrate 3D understanding without sacrificing real-time performance.

Method: R3DP uses asynchronous fast-slow collaboration: queries pre-trained slow system (VGGT) only on sparse key frames while employing lightweight Temporal Feature Prediction Network (TFPNet) to predict features for intermediate frames, plus Multi-View Feature Fuser (MVFF) for effective multi-view fusion with camera intrinsics/extrinsics.

Result: Outperforms single-view and multi-view DP by 32.9% and 51.4% in average success rate respectively; achieves 44.8% reduction in inference time compared to naive DP+VGGT integration.

Conclusion: R3DP provides a plug-and-play solution for integrating large 3D models into real-time inference systems, effectively harnessing 3D priors while maintaining real-time efficiency through temporal feature prediction and multi-view fusion.

Abstract: Embodied manipulation requires accurate 3D understanding of objects and their spatial relations to plan and execute contact-rich actions. While large-scale 3D vision models provide strong priors, their computational cost incurs prohibitive latency for real-time control. We propose Real-time 3D-aware Policy (R3DP), which integrates powerful 3D priors into manipulation policies without sacrificing real-time performance. A core innovation of R3DP is the asynchronous fast-slow collaboration module, which seamlessly integrates large-scale 3D priors into the policy without compromising real-time performance. The system maintains real-time efficiency by querying the pre-trained slow system (VGGT) only on sparse key frames, while simultaneously employing a lightweight Temporal Feature Prediction Network (TFPNet) to predict features for all intermediate frames. By leveraging historical data to exploit temporal correlations, TFPNet explicitly improves task success rates through consistent feature estimation. Additionally, to enable more effective multi-view fusion, we introduce a Multi-View Feature Fuser (MVFF) that aggregates features across views by explicitly incorporating camera intrinsics and extrinsics. R3DP offers a plug-and-play solution for integrating large models into real-time inference systems. We evaluate R3DP against multiple baselines across different visual configurations. R3DP effectively harnesses large-scale 3D priors to achieve superior results, outperforming single-view and multi-view DP by 32.9% and 51.4% in average success rate, respectively. Furthermore, by decoupling heavy 3D reasoning from policy execution, R3DP achieves a 44.8% reduction in inference time compared to a naive DP+VGGT integration.

</details>


### [167] [One-Policy-Fits-All: Geometry-Aware Action Latents for Cross-Embodiment Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14522)
*Juncheng Mu,Sizhe Yang,Hojin Bae,Feiyu Jia,Qingwei Ben,Boyi Li,Huazhe Xu,Jiangmiao Pang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OPFA is a framework that learns a single policy across multiple robot embodiments by creating a shared latent action space and unified decoder, enabling efficient cross-embodiment training and transfer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cross-embodiment manipulation is essential for scaling robot manipulation and reducing data collection costs, but significant differences between embodiments (action spaces, structural disparities) make joint training challenging.

Method: Proposes One-Policy-Fits-All (OPFA) with two key components: 1) Geometry-Aware Latent Representation (GaLR) using 3D convolution networks and transformers to build shared latent action space, and 2) unified latent retargeting decoder that extracts embodiment-specific actions without decoder tuning.

Result: Extensive experiments across 11 different end-effectors show OPFA significantly improves policy performance by leveraging heterogeneous data. Cross-embodiment co-training improves success rates by >50% vs single-source training. With only 8 demonstrations from new embodiment, achieves performance comparable to 72-demonstration model.

Conclusion: OPFA enables end-to-end co-training across diverse embodiments (grippers, dexterous hands with arbitrary DoF), significantly improving data efficiency and reducing skill transfer costs while maintaining a single versatile policy.

Abstract: Cross-embodiment manipulation is crucial for enhancing the scalability of robot manipulation and reducing the high cost of data collection. However, the significant differences between embodiments, such as variations in action spaces and structural disparities, pose challenges for joint training across multiple sources of data. To address this, we propose One-Policy-Fits-All (OPFA), a framework that enables learning a single, versatile policy across multiple embodiments. We first learn a Geometry-Aware Latent Representation (GaLR), which leverages 3D convolution networks and transformers to build a shared latent action space across different embodiments. Then we design a unified latent retargeting decoder that extracts embodiment-specific actions from the latent representations, without any embodiment-specific decoder tuning. OPFA enables end-to-end co-training of data from diverse embodiments, including various grippers and dexterous hands with arbitrary degrees of freedom, significantly improving data efficiency and reducing the cost of skill transfer. We conduct extensive experiments across 11 different end-effectors. The results demonstrate that OPFA significantly improves policy performance in diverse settings by leveraging heterogeneous embodiment data. For instance, cross-embodiment co-training can improve success rates by more than 50% compared to single-source training. Moreover, by adding only a few demonstrations from a new embodiment (e.g., eight), OPFA can achieve performance comparable to that of a well-trained model with 72 demonstrations.

</details>


### [168] [Architecting Autonomy for Safe Microgravity Free-Flyer Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14524)
*Keenan Albee,David C. Sternberg,Alexander Hansson,David Schwartz,Ritwik Majumdar,Oliver Jia-Richards*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes an autonomy framework for small free-flying spacecraft to perform inspection and repair services near orbital outposts like the Lunar Gateway, translating mission requirements into concrete planning and control decisions with safety guarantees.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Small free-flying spacecraft can provide vital EVA services (inspection, repair) for future orbital outposts, but operating near delicate space stations requires formalized autonomy. Existing literature lacks realistic system requirements and their translation into autonomy design decisions for inspection missions.

Method: Transforms general mission requirements into concrete decisions for planning and control autonomy architectures. Considers unique constraints (velocity limits, pointing, keep-in/keep-out zones) and implements mission fallback techniques for hierarchical safety guarantees. Uses SmallSatSim simulation environment to realize the autonomy framework.

Result: Develops a complete autonomy framework with motion planning problem formulation, cost function design, path vs. trajectory control considerations. Provides typical inputs/outputs of planning-control stack, notional system requirements (solve times, propellant use), and reference implementation in SmallSatSim.

Conclusion: The proposed autonomy architecture serves as a blueprint for future small satellite autonomous inspection near mission-critical hardware, advancing beyond existing literature by providing realistic system requirements and translating them into autonomy design decisions for inspection planning and control.

Abstract: Small free-flying spacecraft can provide vital extravehicular activity (EVA) services like inspection and repair for future orbital outposts like the Lunar Gateway. Operating adjacent to delicate space station and microgravity targets, these spacecraft require formalization to describe the autonomy that a free-flyer inspection mission must provide. This work explores the transformation of general mission requirements for this class of free-flyer into a set of concrete decisions for the planning and control autonomy architectures that will power such missions. Flowing down from operator commands for inspection of important regions and mission time-criticality, a motion planning problem emerges that provides the basis for developing autonomy solutions. Unique constraints are considered such as velocity limitations, pointing, and keep-in/keep-out zones, with mission fallback techniques for providing hierarchical safety guarantees under model uncertainties and failure. Planning considerations such as cost function design and path vs. trajectory control are discussed. The typical inputs and outputs of the planning and control autonomy stack of such a mission are also provided. Notional system requirements such as solve times and propellant use are documented to inform planning and control design. The entire proposed autonomy framework for free-flyer inspection is realized in the SmallSatSim simulation environment, providing a reference example of free-flyer inspection autonomy. The proposed autonomy architecture serves as a blueprint for future implementations of small satellite autonomous inspection in proximity to mission-critical hardware, going beyond the existing literature in terms of both (1) providing realistic system requirements for an autonomous inspection mission and (2) translating these requirements into autonomy design decisions for inspection planning and control.

</details>


### [169] [Bots and Blocks: Presenting a project-based approach for robotics education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14529)
*Tobias Geger,Dominique Briechle,Andreas Rausch*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Project-based learning framework for teaching robotics through semester-spanning agile projects in applied computer science, using ROS and practical disassembly automation case studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classic study programs fail to prepare students for industry due to lack of practical experience from solely theoretical lecturing. Need to teach both practical and theoretical skills interactively to improve learning outcomes for modern technologies like robotics.

Method: Project-based learning approach with agile, semester-spanning projects. Students learn through interactive "schools" (mix of lectures and exercises) covering basics first, then progressively work in teams on specific use cases. Framework uses Robot Operating System (ROS) for robot programming, with exemplary project developing disassembly software ecosystem for hardware robots.

Result: Proposed framework enables students to gain practical experience with robotics while learning theoretical foundations. The disassembly automation project provides concrete application context for learning ROS and robot programming through hands-on team work.

Conclusion: Project-based learning with agile semester projects effectively bridges the gap between theoretical education and practical industry requirements for robotics education, preparing students better for real-world challenges through interactive, hands-on experience.

Abstract: To prepare students for upcoming trends and challenges, it is important to teach them about the helpful and important aspects of modern technologies, such as robotics. However, classic study programs often fail to prepare students for working in the industry because of the lack of practical experience, caused by solely theoretical lecturing. The challenge is to teach both practical and theoretical skills interactively to improve the students' learning. In the scope of the paper, a project-based learning approach is proposed, where students are taught in an agile, semester-spanning project how to work with robots. This project is part of the applied computer science degree study program Digital Technologies. The paper presents the framework as well as an exemplary project featuring the development of a disassembly software ecosystem for hardware robots. In the project, the students are taught the programming of robots with the help of the Robot Operating System (ROS). To ensure the base qualifications, the students are taught in so-called schools, an interactive mix of lectures and exercises. At the beginning of the course, the basics of the technologies are covered, while the students work more and more in their team with the robot on a specific use case. The use case here is to automate the disassembly of build block assemblies.

</details>


### [170] [MorFiC: Fixing Value Miscalibration for Zero-Shot Quadruped Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14554)
*Prakhar Mishra,Amir Hossain Raj,Xuesu Xiao,Dinesh Manocha*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: MorFiC enables zero-shot cross-morphology locomotion for quadrupedal robots using a single shared policy with morphology-aware critic conditioning, eliminating per-robot retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current locomotion policies trained on single robots fail when deployed on robots with different morphologies (mass distributions, kinematics, joint limits, actuation constraints), requiring expensive per-robot retraining. A shared critic in multi-morphology training tends to average incompatible value targets across embodiments, leading to miscalibrated advantages and poor transfer performance.

Method: MorFiC uses reinforcement learning with morphology-aware critic conditioning. The critic is modulated via robot physical and control parameters to generate morphology-specific value estimates within a shared network. Trained with a single source robot using morphology randomization in simulation, enabling zero-shot transfer to unseen robots.

Result: MorFiC surpasses morphology-conditioned PPO baselines, improving stable average speed and longest stable run on multiple target robots: +16.1% speed gain on A1, ~2x on Cheetah, and ~5x on B1. Reduces value-prediction error variance across morphologies and stabilizes advantage estimates. Demonstrated zero-shot deployment on Unitree Go1 and Go2 robots without fine-tuning.

Conclusion: Critic-side conditioning with morphology-aware modulation is an effective approach for cross-morphology generalization, enabling zero-shot transfer of locomotion policies across different quadrupedal robots without per-robot retraining.

Abstract: Generalizing learned locomotion policies across quadrupedal robots with different morphologies remain a challenge. Policies trained on a single robot often break when deployed on embodiments with different mass distributions, kinematics, joint limits, or actuation constraints, forcing per robot retraining. We present MorFiC, a reinforcement learning approach for zero-shot cross-morphology locomotion using a single shared policy. MorFiC resolves a key failure mode in multi-morphology actor-critic training: a shared critic tends to average incompatible value targets across embodiments, yielding miscalibrated advantages. To address this, MorFiC conditions the critic via morphology-aware modulation driven by robot physical and control parameters, generating morphology-specific value estimates within a shared network. Trained with a single source robot with morphology randomization in simulation, MorFiC can transfer to unseen robots and surpasses morphology-conditioned PPO baselines by improving stable average speed and longest stable run on multiple targets, including speed gains of +16.1% on A1, ~2x on Cheetah, and ~5x on B1. We additionally show that MorFiC reduces the value-prediction error variance across morphologies and stabilizes the advantage estimates, demonstrating that the improved value-function calibration corresponds to a stronger transfer performance. Finally, we demonstrate zero-shot deployment on two Unitree Go1 and Go2 robots without fine-tuning, indicating that critic-side conditioning is a practical approach for cross-morphology generalization.

</details>


### [171] [SmallSatSim: A High-Fidelity Simulation and Training Toolkit for Microgravity Robotic Close Proximity Operations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14598)
*David Schwartz,Alexander Hansson,Sabrina Bodmer,David Sternberg,Oliver Jia-Richards,Keenan Albee*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SmallSatSim is a high-fidelity simulation toolkit for microgravity rendezvous and proximity operations, built on MuJoCo physics engine with GPU acceleration for testing robotic control algorithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Microgravity RPO operations face unique challenges for robotic control in applications like in-space assembly, debris remediation, and small body exploration. There's a need for accurate simulation tools to develop and test novel RPO algorithms for agile mission scenarios including free-floating manipulation and planning under failure conditions.

Method: Developed SmallSatSim using MuJoCo physics engine to model small satellite RPO dynamics in microgravity robotic free-flight settings. Implemented GPU-accelerated pipeline using MuJoCo MJX and JAX for sampling- and learning-based simulation. Included configurable failure models, visualization, logging, and GPU-enabled parallelization capabilities.

Result: Created a comprehensive simulation toolkit that accurately models RPO dynamics including disturbances and perturbations. The framework includes out-of-the-box free-flyer control techniques and supports evaluation of safe control strategies under adversarial conditions through configurable failure models.

Conclusion: SmallSatSim provides an open-source, high-fidelity simulation platform that accelerates research in autonomous, agile robotic small satellite operations by enabling development and testing of novel RPO algorithms for microgravity environments.

Abstract: Microgravity rendezvous and close proximity operations (RPO) is a growing area of interest for applications spanning in-space assembly and manufacturing (ISAM), orbital debris remediation, and small body exploration. Microgravity environments present unique challenges for robotic control and planning algorithms for new agile RPO mission scenarios like free-floating manipulation, planning under failure, and estimating high-fidelity dynamics of tumbling bodies. To facilitate the development and testing of novel RPO algorithms, we introduce SmallSatSim, a high-fidelity simulation toolkit that leverages the MuJoCo physics engine to accurately model small satellite RPO dynamics in local microgravity robotic free-flight settings, including under model disturbances and perturbations. The framework includes cutting edge out-of-the-box free-flyer control techniques. A GPU-accelerated pipeline using MuJoCo MJX and JAX is implemented for sampling- and learning-based simulation uses cases. SmallSatSim also supports configurable failure models, enabling the evaluation of safe control strategies under adversarial conditions. Visualization, logging, and GPU-enabled parallelization further enhance SmallSatSim's capability for RPO testing. We outline SmallSatSim's features and intended use cases, and demonstrate its use for robotic RPO planning and control. The open-sourced toolkit aims to accelerate research in autonomous, agile robotic small satellite operations.

</details>


### [172] [Latent Dynamics-Aware OOD Monitoring for Trajectory Prediction with Provable Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14603)
*Tongfei Guo,Lili Su*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes a quickest changepoint detection framework for OOD monitoring in trajectory prediction using HMM-based error modeling and cumulative MMD, achieving provable guarantees on detection delay and false alarms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deep learning trajectory prediction models degrade under OOD scenarios in safety-critical CPS, requiring reliable OOD detection with formal guarantees on detection delay and false-alarm rates for real-world deployment.

Method: Formulates OOD monitoring as QCD problem, models ID prediction errors with HMM, extends cumulative MMD approach to detect changes without requiring post-change distribution knowledge while maintaining provable guarantees.

Result: Experiments on three real-world driving datasets show reduced detection delay and robustness to heavy-tailed errors and unknown post-change conditions compared to existing approaches.

Conclusion: The proposed HMM-based QCD framework provides a principled statistical approach for OOD monitoring in trajectory prediction with formal guarantees, addressing reliability concerns in safety-critical CPS applications.

Abstract: In safety-critical Cyber-Physical Systems (CPS), accurate trajectory prediction provides vital guidance for downstream planning and control, yet although deep learning models achieve high-fidelity forecasts on validation data, their reliability degrades under out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios caused by environmental uncertainty or rare traffic behaviors in real-world deployment; detecting such OOD events is challenging due to evolving traffic conditions and changing interaction patterns, while safety-critical applications demand formal guarantees on detection delay and false-alarm rates, motivating us-following recent work [1]-to formulate OOD monitoring for trajectory prediction as a quickest changepoint detection (QCD) problem that offers a principled statistical framework with established theory; we further observe that the real-world evolution of prediction errors under in-distribution (ID) conditions can be effectively modeled by a Hidden Markov Model (HMM), and by leveraging this structure we extend the cumulative Maximum Mean Discrepancy approach to enable detection without requiring explicit knowledge of the post-change distribution while still admitting provable guarantees on delay and false alarms, with experiments on three real-world driving datasets demonstrating reduced detection delay and robustness to heavy-tailed errors and unknown post-change conditions.

</details>


### [173] [Tactile Modality Fusion for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14604)
*Charlotte Morissette,Amin Abyaneh,Wei-Di Chang,Anas Houssaini,David Meger,Hsiu-Chin Lin,Jonathan Tremblay,Gregory Dudek*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: TacFiLM is a lightweight modality-fusion method that integrates visual-tactile signals into vision-language-action models using feature-wise linear modulation, improving contact-rich manipulation performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-language-action models rely primarily on vision, which cannot capture complex interaction dynamics during contact-rich manipulation (forces, friction, compliance, shear). Existing tactile integration methods increase complexity through token concatenation or large-scale pretraining, but behavioral models require more lightweight fusion strategies.

Method: Post-training finetuning approach that conditions intermediate visual features on pretrained tactile representations using feature-wise linear modulation (FiLM). This lightweight fusion strategy integrates tactile signals without heavy computational overhead.

Result: Experimental results on insertion tasks show consistent improvements in success rate, direct insertion performance, completion time, and force stability across both in-distribution and out-of-distribution tasks.

Conclusion: TacFiLM provides an effective approach to integrating tactile signals into VLA models, enhancing contact-rich manipulation behaviors through lightweight modality fusion.

Abstract: We propose TacFiLM, a lightweight modality-fusion approach that integrates visual-tactile signals into vision-language-action (VLA) models. While recent advances in VLA models have introduced robot policies that are both generalizable and semantically grounded, these models mainly rely on vision-based perception. Vision alone, however, cannot capture the complex interaction dynamics that occur during contact-rich manipulation, including contact forces, surface friction, compliance, and shear. While recent attempts to integrate tactile signals into VLA models often increase complexity through token concatenation or large-scale pretraining, the heavy computational demands of behavioural models necessitate more lightweight fusion strategies. To address these challenges, TacFiLM outlines a post-training finetuning approach that conditions intermediate visual features on pretrained tactile representations using feature-wise linear modulation (FiLM). Experimental results on insertion tasks demonstrate consistent improvements in success rate, direct insertion performance, completion time, and force stability across both in-distribution and out-of-distribution tasks. Together, these results support our method as an effective approach to integrating tactile signals into VLA models, improving contact-rich manipulation behaviours.

</details>


### [174] [CyboRacket: A Perception-to-Action Framework for Humanoid Racket Sports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14605)
*Peng Ren,Chuan Qi,Haoyang Ge,Qiyuan Su,Xuguo He,Cong Huang,Pei Chi,Jiang Zhao,Kai Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CyboRacket: A hierarchical perception-action framework for humanoid racket sports using onboard vision, physics-based trajectory prediction, and pre-trained whole-body control for real-time tennis hitting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dynamic ball-interaction tasks are challenging for robots due to tight perception-action coupling under limited reaction time. Existing robotic racket-sport systems rely on external motion capture or task-specific controllers that require retraining across tasks and platforms.

Method: Hierarchical perception-to-action framework integrating onboard visual perception, physics-based trajectory prediction, and large-scale pre-trained whole-body control. Uses onboard cameras to track objects, predicts future trajectories, and converts interception states into target end-effector and base-motion commands for whole-body execution on Unitree G1 humanoid robot with SONIC controller.

Result: Experimental evaluation in vision-based humanoid tennis-hitting task demonstrates real-time visual tracking, trajectory prediction, and successful striking using purely onboard sensing.

Conclusion: CyboRacket presents a unified framework for humanoid racket sports that eliminates dependency on external motion capture and task-specific controllers, enabling real-time performance with onboard sensing.

Abstract: Dynamic ball-interaction tasks remain challenging for robots because they require tight perception-action coupling under limited reaction time. This challenge is especially pronounced in humanoid racket sports, where successful interception depends on accurate visual tracking, trajectory prediction, coordinated stepping, and stable whole-body striking. Existing robotic racket-sport systems often rely on external motion capture for state estimation or on task-specific low-level controllers that must be retrained across tasks and platforms. We present CyboRacket, a hierarchical perception-to-action framework for humanoid racket sports that integrates onboard visual perception, physics-based trajectory prediction, and large-scale pre-trained whole-body control. The framework uses onboard cameras to track the incoming object, predicts its future trajectory, and converts the estimated interception state into target end-effector and base-motion commands for whole-body execution by SONIC on the Unitree G1 humanoid robot. We evaluate the proposed framework in a vision-based humanoid tennis-hitting task. Experimental results demonstrate real-time visual tracking, trajectory prediction, and successful striking using purely onboard sensing.

</details>


### [175] [Physically Accurate Rigid-Body Dynamics in Particle-Based Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14634)
*Ava Abderezaei,Nataliya Nechyporenko,Joseph Miceli,Gilberto Briscoe-Martinez,Alessandro Roncone*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PBD-R is a revised position-based dynamics formulation that enforces physically accurate rigid-body dynamics through momentum conservation constraints, enabling particle-based simulation with improved accuracy for robotics applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotics requires simulation of diverse physical interactions (rigid, deformable, fluids), but current simulators use compositional architectures with multiple subsolvers that complicate physical reasoning. Particle-based simulators offer unified formulation but lack physical accuracy, limiting adoption in robotics.

Method: Introduces PBD-R, a revised PBD formulation with: 1) novel momentum-conservation constraint, 2) modified velocity update to enforce physically accurate rigid-body dynamics. Also creates a solver-agnostic benchmark with analytical solutions for evaluation.

Result: PBD-R significantly outperforms standard PBD in physical accuracy and achieves competitive accuracy with MuJoCo while requiring less computation, as demonstrated using the new benchmark.

Conclusion: PBD-R enables particle-based simulation to meet robotics' physical fidelity demands by improving PBD's accuracy while maintaining computational efficiency, offering a unified alternative to compositional simulators.

Abstract: Robotics demands simulation that can reason about the diversity of real-world physical interactions, from rigid to deformable objects and fluids. Current simulators address this by stitching together multiple subsolvers for different material types, resulting in a compositional architecture that complicates physical reasoning. Particle-based simulators offer a compelling alternative, representing all materials through a single unified formulation that enables seamless cross-material interactions. Among particle-based simulators, position-based dynamics (PBD) is a popular solver known for its computational efficiency and visual plausibility. However, its lack of physical accuracy has limited its adoption in robotics. To leverage the benefits of particle-based solvers while meeting the physical fidelity demands of robotics, we introduce PBD-R, a revised PBD formulation that enforces physically accurate rigid-body dynamics through a novel momentum-conservation constraint and a modified velocity update. Additionally, we introduce a solver-agnostic benchmark with analytical solutions to evaluate physical accuracy. Using this benchmark, we show that PBD-R significantly outperforms PBD and achieves competitive accuracy with MuJoCo while requiring less computation.

</details>


### [176] [Seeing Where to Deploy: Metric RGB-Based Traversability Analysis for Aerial-to-Ground Hidden Space Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14639)
*Seoyoung Lee,Shaekh Mohammad Shithil,Durgakant Pushp,Lantao Liu,Zhangyang Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Aerial-ground cooperation framework for hidden space inspection using metric RGB-based geometric-semantic reconstruction and traversability analysis to identify suitable UGV deployment zones from aerial observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inspecting confined infrastructure like culverts requires accessing hidden spaces with entrances only reachable from elevated viewpoints. Aerial-ground cooperation enables UAV deployment of UGVs, but selecting suitable deployment regions from aerial observations faces challenges of scale ambiguity, reconstruction uncertainty, and terrain semantics.

Method: Multi-view RGB reconstruction backbone produces dense geometry with temporally consistent semantic segmentation for 3D semantic mapping. Embodied motion prior recovers metric scale by enforcing consistency between predicted camera motion and onboard platform egomotion. Confidence-aware geometric-semantic traversability map is constructed from metrically grounded reconstruction to evaluate candidate deployment zones under explicit reachability constraints.

Result: Experiments on a tethered UAV-UGV platform demonstrate reliable deployment-zone identification in hidden space scenarios without requiring LiDAR-based dense mapping.

Conclusion: The framework enables metric terrain reasoning for aerial-to-ground hidden space inspection by addressing scale ambiguity, reconstruction uncertainty, and terrain semantics through RGB-based reconstruction with embodied motion priors and semantic analysis.

Abstract: Inspection of confined infrastructure such as culverts often requires accessing hidden spaces whose entrances are reachable primarily from elevated viewpoints. Aerial-ground cooperation enables a UAV to deploy a compact UGV for interior exploration, but selecting a suitable deployment region from aerial observations requires metric terrain reasoning involving scale ambiguity, reconstruction uncertainty, and terrain semantics. We present a metric RGB-based geometric-semantic reconstruction and traversability analysis framework for aerial-to-ground hidden space inspection. A feed-forward multi-view RGB reconstruction backbone produces dense geometry, while temporally consistent semantic segmentation yields a 3D semantic map. To enable deployment-relevant measurements without LiDAR-based dense mapping, we introduce an embodied motion prior that recovers metric scale by enforcing consistency between predicted camera motion and onboard platform egomotion. From the metrically grounded reconstruction, we construct a confidence-aware geometric-semantic traversability map and evaluate candidate deployment zones under explicit reachability constraints. Experiments on a tethered UAV-UGV platform demonstrate reliable deployment-zone identification in hidden space scenarios.

</details>


### [177] [Coordinate-Independent Robot Model Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14656)
*Yanhao Yang,Ross L. Hatton*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes coordinate-independent robot model identification using dual metric weighting of inverse-dynamics residuals, eliminating coordinate-induced bias while maintaining convexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing robot model identification methods using least-squares regression on inverse dynamics measure residuals directly in coordinate force space, making them dependent on coordinate chart choice, units, and scaling, which introduces bias.

Method: Uses coordinate-independent identification that weights inverse-dynamics residuals by the dual metric induced by the system Riemannian metric. Leverages force-velocity vector-covector duality to provide physically meaningful normalization of generalized forces, pulling coordinate residuals back into ambient mechanical space. Maintains convexity through affine-metric and Schur-complement reformulation, compatible with physical-consistency constraints and geometric regularization.

Result: Experiments on inertia-dominated Crazyflie-pendulum system and drag-dominated LandSalp robot show improved identification accuracy, especially on shape coordinates, in both low-data and high-data settings.

Conclusion: The proposed coordinate-independent identification method eliminates coordinate-induced bias while maintaining convex optimization properties, leading to improved model identification accuracy across different robot types and data regimes.

Abstract: Robot model identification is commonly performed by least-squares regression on inverse dynamics, but existing formulations measure residuals directly in coordinate force space and therefore depend on the chosen coordinate chart, units, and scaling. This paper proposes a coordinate-independent identification method that weights inverse-dynamics residuals by the dual metric induced by the system Riemannian metric. Using the force--velocity vector--covector duality, the dual metric provides a physically meaningful normalization of generalized forces, pulling coordinate residuals back into the ambient mechanical space and eliminating coordinate-induced bias. The resulting objective remains convex through an affine-metric and Schur-complement reformulation, and is compatible with physical-consistency constraints and geometric regularization. Experiments on an inertia-dominated Crazyflie--pendulum system and a drag-dominated LandSalp robot show improved identification accuracy, especially on shape coordinates, in both low-data and high-data settings.

</details>


### [178] [A Dual Quaternion Framework for Collision Recovery of Quadrotor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14698)
*Valentin Gaucher,Wenlong Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A dual quaternion reset map for rigid-body impacts on SE(3) manifold that couples linear and angular impulses, enabling consistent state transitions and improved impact recovery control for UAVs in cluttered environments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional contact models decouple linear and angular impulses, risking manifold inconsistency during rapid state transitions for UAVs operating in cluttered environments that require accurate impact modeling to maintain stability.

Method: Presents a dual quaternion reset map that resolves rigid-body impacts directly on the SE(3) manifold, operating on unified spatial twist (linear and angular velocities as single dual entity). This formulation is algebraically equivalent to classical Newton impulse model while preserving manifold consistency. Builds on this framework to design a hybrid recovery controller that couples linear and angular momentum for strict energy dissipation across impacts.

Result: Hardware-in-the-loop benchmarks show 24% reduction in execution latency compared to optimized matrix-based implementation. High-fidelity MuJoCo simulations demonstrate controller robustness to complex contact dynamics: 56.6% reduction in post-impact RMSE and 41.2% decrease in peak kinetic energy compared to decoupled recovery methods.

Conclusion: The dual quaternion reset map provides a manifold-consistent approach to rigid-body impact modeling that significantly improves UAV stability and recovery performance in cluttered environments through coupled linear-angular impulse handling and energy dissipation.

Abstract: Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) operating in cluttered environments require accurate impact modeling to maintain stability. However, conventional contact models decouple linear and angular impulses, risking manifold inconsistency during rapid state transitions. This article presents a dual quaternion reset map that resolves rigid-body impacts directly on the SE(3) manifold. By operating on the unified spatial twist (linear and angular velocities as a single dual entity), our formulation is algebraically equivalent to the classical Newton impulse model while preserving manifold consistency during discrete state jumps. Building on this framework, we design a hybrid recovery controller that couples linear and angular momentum to ensure strict energy dissipation across impacts. Hardware-in-the-loop benchmarks demonstrate a 24% reduction in execution latency compared to an optimized matrix-based implementation. High-fidelity MuJoCo simulations validate the controller's robustness to complex contact dynamics, showing a 56.6% reduction in post-impact root-mean-square error (RMSE) and a 41.2% decrease in peak kinetic energy compared to decoupled recovery methods.

</details>


### [179] [LiDAR-EVS: Enhance Extrapolated View Synthesis for 3D Gaussian Splatting with Pseudo-LiDAR Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14763)
*Yiming Huang,Xin Kang,Sipeng Zhang,Hongliang Ren,Weihua Zhang,Junjie Lai*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LiDAR-EVS: A lightweight plug-and-play framework for robust extrapolated-view LiDAR simulation in autonomous driving using 3D Gaussian Splatting, addressing overfitting and poor generalization to novel driving trajectories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3D Gaussian Splatting struggles with LiDAR simulation for extrapolated views beyond training trajectories due to overfitting on single-traversal scans and poor generalization to novel ego-vehicle paths, limiting reliable simulation without external multi-pass data.

Method: Two key components: (1) pseudo extrapolated-view point cloud supervision with multi-frame LiDAR fusion, view transformation, occlusion curling, and intensity adjustment; (2) spatially-constrained dropout regularization for robustness to real-world trajectory variations.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on extrapolated-view LiDAR synthesis across three datasets, demonstrating effectiveness for novel driving trajectories without requiring external multi-pass data.

Conclusion: LiDAR-EVS is a promising lightweight framework for robust LiDAR simulation in autonomous driving applications, enabling data-driven simulation, closed-loop evaluation, and synthetic data generation for unseen driving trajectories.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a powerful technique for real-time LiDAR and camera synthesis in autonomous driving simulation. However, simulating LiDAR with 3DGS remains challenging for extrapolated views beyond the training trajectory, as existing methods are typically trained on single-traversal sensor scans, suffer from severe overfitting and poor generalization to novel ego-vehicle paths. To enable reliable simulation of LiDAR along unseen driving trajectories without external multi-pass data, we present LiDAR-EVS, a lightweight framework for robust extrapolated-view LiDAR simulation in autonomous driving. Designed to be plug-and-play, LiDAR-EVS readily extends to diverse LiDAR sensors and neural rendering baselines with minimal modification. Our framework comprises two key components: (1) pseudo extrapolated-view point cloud supervision with multi-frame LiDAR fusion, view transformation, occlusion curling, and intensity adjustment; (2) spatially-constrained dropout regularization that promotes robustness to diverse trajectory variations encountered in real-world driving. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LiDAR-EVS achieves SOTA performance on extrapolated-view LiDAR synthesis across three datasets, making it a promising tool for data-driven simulation, closed-loop evaluation, and synthetic data generation in autonomous driving systems.

</details>


### [180] [CORAL: COntextual Reasoning And Local Planning in A Hierarchical VLM Framework for Underwater Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14786)
*Zhenqi Wu,Yuanjie Lu,Xuesu Xiao,Xiaomin Lin*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CORAL framework decouples semantic reasoning from reactive control for AUV reef monitoring, using VLMs for high-level waypoint selection and dynamics-based planners for low-level execution, improving coverage by 14.28% while eliminating collisions and reducing VLM calls by 57%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Oyster reef restoration requires underwater monitoring that is costly and hazardous for human divers. Existing AUVs lack semantic reasoning capabilities, while current VLM-driven systems have three key limitations: frequent inference waits, inability to model robot dynamics causing collisions, and accumulation of path errors due to limited self-correction.

Method: CORAL framework decouples high-level semantic reasoning from low-level reactive control. Vision-language models provide high-level exploration guidance by selecting waypoints, while a dynamics-based planner handles low-level collision-free execution. A geometric verification module validates waypoints and triggers replanning when needed.

Result: Compared with previous state-of-the-art, CORAL improves coverage by 14.28 percentage points (17.85% relative improvement), reduces collisions by 100% (eliminates all collisions), and requires 57% fewer VLM calls.

Conclusion: The CORAL framework successfully addresses limitations of end-to-end VLM-driven systems by separating semantic reasoning from reactive control, enabling more efficient and safer autonomous underwater monitoring of oyster reefs while reducing computational overhead.

Abstract: Oyster reefs are critical ecosystem species that sustain biodiversity, filter water, and protect coastlines, yet they continue to decline globally. Restoring these ecosystems requires regular underwater monitoring to assess reef health, a task that remains costly, hazardous, and limited when performed by human divers. Autonomous underwater vehicles (AUVs) offer a promising alternative, but existing AUVs rely on geometry-based navigation that cannot interpret scene semantics. Recent vision-language models (VLMs) enable semantic reasoning for intelligent exploration, but existing VLM-driven systems adopt an end-to-end paradigm, introducing three key limitations. First, these systems require the VLM to generate every navigation decision, forcing frequent waits for inference. Second, VLMs cannot model robot dynamics, causing collisions in cluttered environments. Third, limited self-correction allows small deviations to accumulate into large path errors. To address these limitations, we propose CORAL, a framework that decouples high-level semantic reasoning from low-level reactive control. The VLM provides high-level exploration guidance by selecting waypoints, while a dynamics-based planner handles low-level collision-free execution. A geometric verification module validates waypoints and triggers replanning when needed. Compared with the previous state-of-the-art, CORAL improves coverage by 14.28% percentage points, or 17.85% relatively, reduces collisions by 100%, and requires 57% fewer VLM calls.

</details>


### [181] [Exploring the dynamic properties and motion reproducibility of a small upper-body humanoid robot with 13-DOF pneumatic actuation for data-driven control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14787)
*Hiroshi Atsuta,Hisashi Ishihara,Minoru Asada*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A 13-DOF pneumatic upper-body humanoid robot was developed, with a data-driven MLP controller outperforming PID for trajectory tracking by leveraging system reproducibility and time delay compensation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pneumatically-actuated anthropomorphic robots with high DOF have great potential for physical human-robot interaction, but precise control is challenging due to inherent nonlinearities in pneumatic actuators.

Method: Developed a compact 13-DOF upper-body humanoid robot, investigated its dynamic properties (actuation time delays), confirmed system reproducibility, and implemented a data-driven multilayer perceptron controller with explicit time delay compensation for a 4-DOF arm subsystem trained on random movement data.

Result: The data-driven MLP controller demonstrated superior trajectory tracking performance compared to a traditional PID controller, highlighting the effectiveness of data-driven approaches for complex pneumatic systems.

Conclusion: Data-driven approaches show significant potential for controlling complex, high-DOF pneumatic robots, with the MLP-based controller outperforming traditional PID methods by effectively handling system nonlinearities and time delays.

Abstract: Pneumatically-actuated anthropomorphic robots with high degrees of freedom (DOF) offer significant potential for physical human-robot interaction. However, precise control of pneumatic actuators is challenging due to their inherent nonlinearities. This paper presents the development of a compact 13-DOF upper-body humanoid robot. To assess the feasibility of an effective controller, we first investigate its key dynamic properties, such as actuation time delays, and confirm that the system exhibits highly reproducible behavior. Leveraging this reproducibility, we implement a preliminary data-driven controller for a 4-DOF arm subsystem based on a multilayer perceptron with explicit time delay compensation. The network was trained on random movement data to generate pressure commands for tracking arbitrary trajectories. Comparative evaluations with a traditional PID controller demonstrate superior trajectory tracking performance, highlighting the potential of data-driven approaches for controlling complex, high-DOF pneumatic robots.

</details>


### [182] [GraspALL: Adaptive Structural Compensation from Illumination Variation for Robotic Garment Grasping in Any Low-Light Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14789)
*Haifeng Zhong,Wenshuo Han,Zhouyu Wang,Runyang Feng,Fan Tang,Tong-Yee Lee,Zipei Fan,Ruihai Wu,Yuran Wang,Hao Dong,Hechang Chen,Hyung Jin Chang,Yixing Gao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: GraspALL is an illumination-structure interactive compensation model that improves garment grasping accuracy under varying lighting conditions by adaptively fusing RGB and non-RGB features based on continuous illumination changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing garment grasping methods fail under dynamically changing illumination, especially in low-light scenes where reduced illumination degrades garment structural features. Current multimodal approaches overlook the varying dependence on non-RGB features under different lighting conditions, introducing misaligned cues that weaken illumination adaptability.

Method: GraspALL encodes continuous illumination changes into quantitative references to guide adaptive feature fusion between RGB and non-RGB modalities according to varying lighting intensities, generating illumination-consistent grasping representations through illumination-structure interactive compensation.

Result: Experiments on a self-built garment grasping dataset show GraspALL improves grasping accuracy by 32-44% over baselines under diverse illumination conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed illumination-structure interactive compensation model effectively addresses the challenge of garment grasping under dynamically changing illumination by adaptively fusing multimodal features based on quantitative illumination references.

Abstract: Achieving accurate garment grasping under dynamically changing illumination is crucial for all-day operation of service robots.However, the reduced illumination in low-light scenes severely degrades garment structural features, leading to a significant drop in grasping robustness.Existing methods typically enhance RGB features by exploiting the illumination-invariant properties of non-RGB modalities, yet they overlook the varying dependence on non-RGB features under varying lighting conditions, which can introduce misaligned non-RGB cues and thereby weaken the model's adaptability to illumination changes when utilizing multimodal information.To address this problem, we propose GraspALL, an illumination-structure interactive compensation model.The innovation of GraspALL lies in encoding continuous illumination changes into quantitative references to guide adaptive feature fusion between RGB and non-RGB modalities according to varying lighting intensities, thereby generating illumination-consistent grasping representations.Experiments on the self-built garment grasping dataset demonstrate that GraspALL improves grasping accuracy by 32-44% over baselines under diverse illumination conditions.

</details>


### [183] [A Unified Calibration Framework for Coordinate and Kinematic Parameters in Dual-Arm Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14809)
*Tianyu Huang,Bohan Yang,Bin Li,Wenpan Li,Haoang Li,Wenlong Li,Yun-Hui Liu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A unified calibration framework for dual-arm robots that jointly optimizes coordinate transformations and kinematic parameters using Lie-algebraic formulation to minimize error propagation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing dual-robot calibration methods either treat kinematic errors as implicit noise or handle them through separated error modeling, leading to non-negligible accumulated errors that affect precise collaboration in vision-based dual-arm systems.

Method: Proposes a unified calibration framework using Lie-algebraic formulation based on product-of-exponentials formula. Constructs consolidated error model integrating coordinate and kinematic parameters in twist forms, derives closed-form analytical Jacobian using Lie derivatives, analyzes parameter identifiability, and develops certifiably correct initialization algorithm using semidefinite relaxation.

Result: Extensive experiments show superior accuracy over previous baselines under identical visual measurements. The certifiable initialization consistently outperforms coordinate-only baselines and provides reliable starting points for joint optimization.

Conclusion: The unified calibration framework effectively mitigates error propagation by avoiding artificial error separation, enables stable optimization on manifold space, and provides certifiably correct initialization for robust convergence, achieving more accurate calibration for vision-based dual-arm robot systems.

Abstract: Precise collaboration in vision-based dual-arm robot systems requires accurate system calibration. Recent dual-robot calibration methods have achieved strong performance by simultaneously solving multiple coordinate transformations. However, these methods either treat kinematic errors as implicit noise or handle them through separated error modeling, resulting in non-negligible accumulated errors. In this paper, we present a novel framework for unified calibration of the coordinate transformations and kinematic parameters in both robot arms. Our key idea is to unify all the tightly coupled parameters within a single Lie-algebraic formulation. To this end, we construct a consolidated error model grounded in the product-of-exponentials formula, which naturally integrates the coordinate and kinematic parameters in twist forms. Our model introduces no artificial error separation and thus greatly mitigates the error propagation. In addition, we derive a closed-form analytical Jacobian from this model using Lie derivatives. By exploring the Jacobian rank property, we analyze the identifiability of all calibration parameters and show that our joint optimization is well-posed under mild conditions. This enables off-the-shelf iterative solvers to stably optimize these parameters on the manifold space. Besides, to ensure robust convergence of our joint optimization, we develop a certifiably correct algorithm for initializing the unknown coordinates. Relying on semidefinite relaxation, our algorithm can yield a reliable estimate whose near-global optimality can be verified a posteriori. Extensive experiments validate the superior accuracy of our approach over previous baselines under identical visual measurements. Meanwhile, our certifiable initialization consistently outperforms several coordinate-only baselines, proving its reliability as a starting point for joint optimization.

</details>


### [184] [Ego to World: Collaborative Spatial Reasoning in Embodied Systems via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14811)
*Heng Zhou,Li Kang,Yiran Qin,Xiufeng Song,Ao Yu,Zilu Zhang,Haoming Song,Kaixin Xu,Yuchen Fan,Dongzhan Zhou,Xiaohong Liu,Ruimao Zhang,Philip Torr,Lei Bai,Zhenfei Yin*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CoRL framework combines Chain-of-Thought supervised fine-tuning with reinforcement learning using Group-Relative Policy Optimization to enable vision-language models to fuse heterogeneous ego-centric viewpoints for world-centric scene understanding, validated on the new E2W benchmark.


<details>
