Introduce a two-stage grasp data generation strategy: dense single-object grasp synthesis followed by dense scene-level grasp generation, with explicit modeling of grasp offsets and pre-grasp configurations.

Result: DGS-Net achieves 88.63% grasp success rate in simulation and 78.98% on real robotic platform, with low penetration (0.375 mm mean depth, 559.45 mm³ volume), outperforming existing methods. Dataset includes 307 objects, 240 multi-object scenes, and over 350k validated grasps.

Conclusion: The proposed approach demonstrates strong effectiveness and generalization capability for dexterous grasping in multi-object scenes, addressing limitations of existing datasets through explicit modeling of grasp offsets and pre-grasp configurations.

Abstract: Dexterous grasping in multi-object scene constitutes a fundamental challenge in robotic manipulation. Current mainstream grasping datasets predominantly focus on single-object scenarios and predefined grasp configurations, often neglecting environmental interference and the modeling of dexterous pre-grasp gesture, thereby limiting their generalizability in real-world applications. To address this, we propose DGS-Net, an end-to-end grasp prediction network capable of learning dense grasp configurations from single-view point clouds in multi-object scene. Furthermore, we propose a two-stage grasp data generation strategy that progresses from dense single-object grasp synthesis to dense scene-level grasp generation. Our dataset comprises 307 objects, 240 multi-object scenes, and over 350k validated grasps. By explicitly modeling grasp offsets and pre-grasp configurations, the dataset provides more robust and accurate supervision for dexterous grasp learning. Experimental results show that DGS-Net achieves grasp success rates of 88.63\% in simulation and 78.98\% on a real robotic platform, while exhibiting lower penetration with a mean penetration depth of 0.375 mm and penetration volume of 559.45 mm^3, outperforming existing methods and demonstrating strong effectiveness and generalization capability. Our dataset is available at https://github.com/4taotao8/DGS-Net.

</details>


### [212] [MA-VLCM: A Vision Language Critic Model for Value Estimation of Policies in Multi-Agent Team Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15418)
*Shahil Shaik,Aditya Parameshwaran,Anshul Nayak,Jonathon M. Smereka,Yue Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: MA-VLCM replaces learned centralized critics in MARL with fine-tuned vision-language models for multi-agent behavior evaluation, improving sample efficiency and enabling deployment on resource-constrained robots.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional MARL centralized critics are sample-inefficient and lack generalization, while VLAs have strong reasoning capabilities but are computationally prohibitive for direct deployment in heterogeneous multi-robot systems with diverse embodiments and resource constraints.

Method: Proposes Multi-Agent Vision-Language-Critic Models (MA-VLCM) that replace learned centralized critics with pretrained vision-language models fine-tuned to evaluate multi-agent behavior. The critic is conditioned on natural language task descriptions, visual trajectory observations, and structured multi-agent state information.

Result: The approach significantly improves sample efficiency by eliminating critic learning during policy optimization, produces compact execution policies suitable for resource-constrained robots, and shows good zero-shot return estimation across different VLM backbones in both in-distribution and out-of-distribution multi-agent team scenarios.

Conclusion: MA-VLCM successfully bridges the gap between sample-inefficient MARL critics and computationally prohibitive VLAs by leveraging pretrained vision-language models as centralized critics, enabling efficient multi-agent learning and deployment in heterogeneous robotic systems.

Abstract: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) commonly relies on a centralized critic to estimate the value function. However, learning such a critic from scratch is highly sample-inefficient and often lacks generalization across environments. At the same time, large vision-language-action models (VLAs) trained on internet-scale data exhibit strong multimodal reasoning and zero-shot generalization capabilities, yet directly deploying them for robotic execution remains computationally prohibitive, particularly in heterogeneous multi-robot systems with diverse embodiments and resource constraints. To address these challenges, we propose Multi-Agent Vision-Language-Critic Models (MA-VLCM), a framework that replaces the learned centralized critic in MARL with a pretrained vision-language model fine-tuned to evaluate multi-agent behavior. MA-VLCM acts as a centralized critic conditioned on natural language task descriptions, visual trajectory observations, and structured multi-agent state information. By eliminating critic learning during policy optimization, our approach significantly improves sample efficiency while producing compact execution policies suitable for deployment on resource-constrained robots. Results show good zero-shot return estimation on models with differing VLM backbones on in-distribution and out-of-distribution scenarios in multi-agent team settings

</details>


### [213] [Zero-Shot Generalization from Motion Demonstrations to New Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15445)
*Kilian Freitag,Alvin Combrink,Nadia Figueroa*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper introduces Gaussian Graph to combine isolated motion demonstrations into a graph structure, enabling generalization to unseen tasks through Stitching and Chaining frameworks while maintaining stability guarantees.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing dynamical systems (DS) learning methods model isolated tasks and struggle to reuse demonstrations for novel behaviors, despite providing fast, reactive, and provably stable control from few demonstrations. There's a need to combine isolated demonstrations within shared workspaces to enable generalization to unseen tasks.

Method: Introduces Gaussian Graph which reinterprets spatial components of learned motion primitives as discrete vertices with connections. This bridges continuous control with discrete graph search. Two frameworks are proposed: Stitching for constructing time-invariant DSs, and Chaining for sequence-based DSs for complex motions while retaining convergence guarantees.

Result: Simulations and real-robot experiments show that these methods successfully generalize to new tasks where baseline methods fail, demonstrating effective reuse of demonstrations for novel behaviors.

Conclusion: The Gaussian Graph formulation enables combining isolated demonstrations into a shared representation that supports generalization to unseen tasks while maintaining the computational efficiency and stability guarantees of DS learning approaches.

Abstract: Learning motion policies from expert demonstrations is an essential paradigm in modern robotics. While end-to-end models aim for broad generalization, they require large datasets and computationally heavy inference. Conversely, learning dynamical systems (DS) provides fast, reactive, and provably stable control from very few demonstrations. However, existing DS learning methods typically model isolated tasks and struggle to reuse demonstrations for novel behaviors. In this work, we formalize the problem of combining isolated demonstrations within a shared workspace to enable generalization to unseen tasks. The Gaussian Graph is introduced, which reinterprets spatial components of learned motion primitives as discrete vertices with connections to one another. This formulation allows us to bridge continuous control with discrete graph search. We propose two frameworks leveraging this graph: Stitching, for constructing time-invariant DSs, and Chaining, giving a sequence-based DS for complex motions while retaining convergence guarantees. Simulations and real-robot experiments show that these methods successfully generalize to new tasks where baseline methods fail.

</details>


### [214] [RoCo Challenge at AAAI 2026: Benchmarking Robotic Collaborative Manipulation for Assembly Towards Industrial Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15469)
*Haichao Liu,Yuheng Zhou,Zhenyu Wu,Ziheng Ji,Ziyu Shan,Qianzhun Wang,Ruixuan Liu,Zhiyuan Yang,Yejun Gu,Shalman Khan,Shijun Yan,Jun Liu,Haiyue Zhu,Changliu Liu,Jianfei Yang,Jingbing Zhang,Ziwei Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The RoCo Challenge introduces a benchmark for robotic assembly assistance with simulation and real-world phases focusing on planetary gearbox assembly, attracting 60+ teams and revealing effective dual-model frameworks and recovery-from-failure strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To benchmark and advance Embodied AI capabilities for industrial robotic manipulation, particularly in human-centered manufacturing, by creating a standardized challenge for high-precision assembly tasks that can free human workers from repetitive, dangerous labor.

Method: Developed a self-contained data collection, training, and evaluation system in Isaac Sim with dual-arm robot deployment. The challenge has two phases: Simulation Round with fine-grained task phases for step-wise scoring, and Real-World Round using physical gearbox components with high-quality teleoperated datasets. Core task involves assembling an epicyclic gearbox from scratch with multiple components.

Result: Attracted over 60 teams and 170+ participants from 10+ countries. Most effective solutions were ARC-VLA and RoboCola. Key findings show dual-model frameworks for long-horizon multi-task learning are highly effective, and strategic utilization of recovery-from-failure curriculum data is critical for successful deployment.

Conclusion: The RoCo Challenge successfully established a benchmark for industrial EAI in assembly manipulation, demonstrating effective approaches for long-horizon tasks and providing insights for future development in human-centered manufacturing robotics.

Abstract: Embodied Artificial Intelligence (EAI) is rapidly developing, gradually subverting previous autonomous systems' paradigms from isolated perception to integrated, continuous action. This transition is highly significant for industrial robotic manipulation, promising to free human workers from repetitive, dangerous daily labor. To benchmark and advance this capability, we introduce the Robotic Collaborative Assembly Assistance (RoCo) Challenge with a dataset towards simulation and real-world assembly manipulation. Set against the backdrop of human-centered manufacturing, this challenge focuses on a high-precision planetary gearbox assembly task, a demanding yet highly representative operation in modern industry. Built upon a self-developed data collection, training, and evaluation system in Isaac Sim, and utilizing a dual-arm robot for real-world deployment, the challenge operates in two phases. The Simulation Round defines fine-grained task phases for step-wise scoring to handle the long-horizon nature of the assembly. The Real-World Round mirrors this evaluation with physical gearbox components and high-quality teleoperated datasets. The core tasks require assembling an epicyclic gearbox from scratch, including mounting three planet gears, a sun gear, and a ring gear. Attracting over 60 teams and 170+ participants from more than 10 countries, the challenge yielded highly effective solutions, most notably ARC-VLA and RoboCola. Results demonstrate that a dual-model framework for long-horizon multi-task learning is highly effective, and the strategic utilization of recovery-from-failure curriculum data is a critical insight for successful deployment. This report outlines the competition setup, evaluation approach, key findings, and future directions for industrial EAI. Our dataset, CAD files, code, and evaluation results can be found at: https://rocochallenge.github.io/RoCo2026/.

</details>


### [215] [On the Derivation of Tightly-Coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry with VoxelMap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15471)
*Zhihao Zhan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A concise mathematical formulation of tightly-coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry using an iterated error-state Kalman filter with VoxelMap representation, providing unified derivation rather than new algorithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a clear, self-contained derivation that unifies geometric modeling and probabilistic state estimation for tightly-coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry, serving as both technical reference and accessible entry point.

Method: Mathematical formulation within an iterated error-state Kalman filter framework using VoxelMap representation, with consistent notation and explicit formulations for geometric modeling and probabilistic state estimation.

Result: A unified derivation that clarifies the system architecture and estimation principles of tightly-coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry, making the underlying mathematics accessible and self-contained.

Conclusion: The note successfully provides a foundational understanding of tightly-coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry through clear mathematical formulation, serving as valuable reference material for researchers and practitioners in the field.

Abstract: This note presents a concise mathematical formulation of tightly-coupled LiDAR-Inertial Odometry within an iterated error-state Kalman filter framework using a VoxelMap representation. Rather than proposing a new algorithm, it provides a clear and self-contained derivation that unifies the geometric modeling and probabilistic state estimation through consistent notation and explicit formulations. The document is intended to serve both as a technical reference and as an accessible entry point for a foundational understanding of the system architecture and estimation principles.

</details>


### [216] [Optimal control of differentially flat underactuated planar robots in the perspective of oscillation mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15528)
*Stefano Lovato,Michele Tonan,Matteo Bottin,Matteo Massaro,Alberto Doria,Giulio Rosati*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Optimal control combined with differential flatness theory improves trajectory tracking for underactuated robots by minimizing residual oscillations through performance indices optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Underactuated robots offer lightweight, cost-effective designs with enhanced dexterity, but controlling passive joints requires precise dynamic modeling. Simplifying models by neglecting friction introduces residual end-effector oscillations around target positions, which needs to be addressed.

Method: Combines optimal control with differential flatness theory to plan flat variables using quadratic performance indices (control effort/motor torque and potential energy of underactuated joint). Formal analysis followed by numerical simulation validation.

Result: Optimal control effectively plans flat variables considering different performance indices. Minimizing potential energy enables design of motion laws robust against stiffness/damping variations in underactuated joints, reducing oscillations from stiffness/damping mismatch.

Conclusion: Optimal control integration with differential flatness theory provides an effective approach to improve trajectory tracking in underactuated robots by addressing residual oscillations through performance optimization, particularly enhancing robustness to parameter variations.

Abstract: Underactuated robots are characterized by a larger number of degrees of freedom than actuators and if they are designed with a specific mass distribution, they can be controlled by means of differential flatness theory. This structural property enables the development of lightweight and cost-effective robotic systems with enhanced dexterity. However, a key challenge lies in managing the passive joints, whose control demands precise and comprehensive dynamic modeling of the system. To simplify dynamic models, particularly for low-speed trajectories, friction is often neglected. While this assumption simplifies analysis and control design, it introduces residual oscillations of the end-effector about the target position. In this paper, the possibility of using optimal control along with differential flatness control is investigated to improve the tracking of the planned trajectories. First, the study was carried out through formal analysis, and then, it was validated by means of numerical simulations. Results highlight that optimal control can be used to plan the flat variables considering different (quadratic) performance indices: control effort, i.e. motor torque, and potential energy of the considered underactuated joint. Moreover, the minimization of potential energy can be used to design motion laws that are robust against variation of the stiffness and damping of the underactuated joint, thus reducing oscillations in the case of stiffness/damping mismatch.

</details>


### [217] [From Passive Observer to Active Critic: Reinforcement Learning Elicits Process Reasoning for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15600)
*Yibin Liu,Yaxing Lyu,Daqi Gao,Zhixuan Liang,Weiliang Tang,Shilong Mu,Xiaokang Yang,Yao Mu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PRIMO R1 transforms video MLLMs from passive observers to active critics for robotic manipulation monitoring using outcome-based RL and structured temporal inputs, achieving SOTA performance with a 7B model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video MLLMs trained via SFT act as passive observers that recognize events rather than evaluating progress toward task goals, creating a bottleneck for accurate process supervision in long-horizon robotic manipulation.

Method: Introduces PRIMO R1 framework that uses outcome-based Reinforcement Learning to incentivize explicit Chain-of-Thought generation for progress estimation, with structured temporal inputs anchoring video sequences between initial and current state images.

Result: Achieves 50% reduction in mean absolute error compared to specialized reasoning baselines, outperforms 72B-scale general MLLMs, and establishes SOTA on RoboFail benchmark with 67.0% accuracy (6.0% above OpenAI o1). Shows strong zero-shot generalization on failure detection tasks.

Conclusion: PRIMO R1 successfully transforms video MLLMs into active critics for process monitoring, demonstrating that outcome-based RL with structured temporal inputs enables effective progress estimation and superior performance in robotic manipulation supervision.

Abstract: Accurate process supervision remains a critical challenge for long-horizon robotic manipulation. A primary bottleneck is that current video MLLMs, trained primarily under a Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) paradigm, function as passive "Observers" that recognize ongoing events rather than evaluating the current state relative to the final task goal. In this paper, we introduce PRIMO R1 (Process Reasoning Induced Monitoring), a 7B framework that transforms video MLLMs into active "Critics". We leverage outcome-based Reinforcement Learning to incentivize explicit Chain-of-Thought generation for progress estimation. Furthermore, our architecture constructs a structured temporal input by explicitly anchoring the video sequence between initial and current state images. Supported by the proposed PRIMO Dataset and Benchmark, extensive experiments across diverse in-domain environments and out-of-domain real-world humanoid scenarios demonstrate that PRIMO R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance. Quantitatively, our 7B model achieves a 50% reduction in the mean absolute error of specialized reasoning baselines, demonstrating significant relative accuracy improvements over 72B-scale general MLLMs. Furthermore, PRIMO R1 exhibits strong zero-shot generalization on difficult failure detection tasks. We establish state-of-the-art performance on RoboFail benchmark with 67.0% accuracy, surpassing closed-source models like OpenAI o1 by 6.0%.

</details>


### [218] [EAAE: Energy-Aware Autonomous Exploration for UAVs in Unknown 3D Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15604)
*Jacob Elskamp,Moji Shi,Leonard Bauersfeld,Davide Scaramuzza,Marija Popović*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: EAAE is a modular frontier-based framework for energy-aware 3D exploration of multirotor UAVs that minimizes energy consumption while maintaining exploration progress through dual-layer planning and power prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard exploration policies for battery-powered multirotor UAVs optimize for coverage or time, leading to energy waste through maneuver-heavy trajectories, limiting mission performance which is often constrained by energy rather than geometry alone.

Method: EAAE clusters frontiers into view-consistent regions, plans dynamically feasible candidate trajectories to informative clusters, predicts execution energy using offline power estimation, and selects targets by minimizing predicted energy while preserving exploration progress through dual-layer planning for safe execution.

Result: EAAE consistently reduces total energy consumption compared to distance-based and information gain-based frontier baselines while maintaining competitive exploration time and comparable map quality across simulated 3D environments of increasing complexity.

Conclusion: EAAE provides a practical drop-in energy-aware layer for frontier exploration that makes energy an explicit decision variable during frontier selection, addressing the energy limitations of battery-powered multirotor UAVs in unknown environment exploration.

Abstract: Battery-powered multirotor unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) can rapidly map unknown environments, but mission performance is often limited by energy rather than geometry alone. Standard exploration policies that optimise for coverage or time can therefore waste energy through manoeuvre-heavy trajectories. In this paper, we address energy-aware autonomous 3D exploration for multirotor UAVs in initially unknown environments. We propose Energy-Aware Autonomous Exploration (EAAE), a modular frontier-based framework that makes energy an explicit decision variable during frontier selection. EAAE clusters frontiers into view-consistent regions, plans dynamically feasible candidate trajectories to the most informative clusters, and predicts their execution energy using an offline power estimation loop. The next target is then selected by minimising predicted trajectory energy while preserving exploration progress through a dual-layer planning architecture for safe execution. We evaluate EAAE in a full exploration pipeline with a rotor-speed-based power model across simulated 3D environments of increasing complexity. Compared to representative distance-based and information gain-based frontier baselines, EAAE consistently reduces total energy consumption while maintaining competitive exploration time and comparable map quality, providing a practical drop-in energy-aware layer for frontier exploration.

</details>


### [219] [Perception-Aware Autonomous Exploration in Feature-Limited Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15605)
*Moji Shi,Rajitha de Silva,Hang Yu,Riccardo Polvara,Marija Popović*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A hierarchical perception-aware exploration framework for UAVs that prioritizes visually informative frontiers and optimizes yaw trajectories to maintain stable visual feature tracking, reducing odometry drift and improving coverage.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing exploration methods focus on coverage efficiency but ignore that VIO performance depends on robust visual features. This can lead robots into feature-sparse regions where tracking degrades, causing odometry drift, corrupted maps, and mission failure.

Method: Hierarchical perception-aware exploration framework with two key components: (1) associates candidate frontiers with expected feature quality using a global feature map to prioritize visually informative subgoals, and (2) optimizes continuous yaw trajectory along planned motion to maintain stable feature tracks.

Result: Method maintains more reliable feature tracking, reduces odometry drift, and achieves on average 30% higher coverage before odometry error exceeds specified thresholds compared to baselines that ignore feature quality and/or don't optimize continuous yaw.

Conclusion: Explicitly coupling exploration progress with feature observability through frontier prioritization and yaw optimization significantly improves VIO reliability and exploration performance in feature-sparse environments.

Abstract: Autonomous exploration in unknown environments typically relies on onboard state estimation for localisation and mapping. Existing exploration methods primarily maximise coverage efficiency, but often overlook that visual-inertial odometry (VIO) performance strongly depends on the availability of robust visual features. As a result, exploration policies can drive a robot into feature-sparse regions where tracking degrades, leading to odometry drift, corrupted maps, and mission failure. We propose a hierarchical perception-aware exploration framework for a stereo-equipped unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) that explicitly couples exploration progress with feature observability. Our approach (i) associates each candidate frontier with an expected feature quality using a global feature map, and prioritises visually informative subgoals, and (ii) optimises a continuous yaw trajectory along the planned motion to maintain stable feature tracks. We evaluate our method in simulation across environments with varying texture levels and in real-world indoor experiments with largely textureless walls. Compared to baselines that ignore feature quality and/or do not optimise continuous yaw, our method maintains more reliable feature tracking, reduces odometry drift, and achieves on average 30\% higher coverage before the odometry error exceeds specified thresholds.

</details>
